- A coding turn's context says whether the session's machine got a new disk since the previous turn began (F3 5g-5 part 3, PR C; migration `20260930515000`, SQL test 536; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-CODING-MACHINES.md` "Reads" rule 4, `unreadable`): a new top-level key `machine: {newDisk}`, true when the machine (the session's `vm_allocation_id`) has a rebuild's `replaced` record at or after the previous turn's begin (its wake occurrence, or when it opened if it never began), false on a first turn. A session not attached when its machine got the new disk has no `MACHINE_NEW_DISK` event, so this is how it learns. The parse takes `machine` as optional, reads a context without it as `{newDisk: false}`, and refuses any other shape (`CODING_TURN_CONTEXT_INVALID`); the coding context carries it, and the instructions add one line, only when it is true, that only `/company` was kept. Today's runtime refuses a context with `machine`, so this runtime goes out before the migration. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-native-coding-runtime.test.ts`.
- Members read mail by direction, and a thread's older messages a page at a time (Interfaces Mailbox, C6e; migration `20260930512000`, SQL test 695; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-EVENT-INTAKE.md`, `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md`). `qorpera_member_mail_threads` takes `direction` (`received` or `sent`): only the threads whose newest message went that way, the `direction` each row shows, kept before the page or a state's window is cut, so `before` and `nextBefore` page through every such thread (new index `qorpera_mail_threads_direction`). `qorpera_member_mail_thread` takes a third argument, `p_before` (`{at, kind, id}`, the `nextBefore` an answer names while `more` is true): the 50 messages before that one, oldest first. Without it the answer is the newest 50, as before; the function is now `(uuid, text, jsonb)` and the two-argument form is gone. Who may call, account mail's hidden parts, the texts and the waiting sends are unchanged.
- A coding session's environment answer also brings the older values used on its machine, for the output's mask only (F3 step 5j-4, review r3 of #1272; migration `20260930514000`, SQL test 613; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-CODING-MACHINES.md` "Secrets, MCP and the vault relay"). Each `environment_secret_used` audit row records the machine the session was attached to (`coding_machine_id`, a plain uuid, null when it had none and on every other event). `qorpera_authorize_coding_environment` also answers `maskSecrets`: the sealed versions used on that machine by a session of the same member, of a secret the vault still serves there (active, the session's environment, a name the vault allows, the member's own or the company's), sealed by this broker under its current key, and none the answer serves; newest use first, at most 64 (`CODING_ENVIRONMENT_MASK_MAXIMUM`) and 256 KiB of envelopes. A revoked secret (Disconnect, or its member's departure) never comes back, and serving them writes no audit row. `CloudSecretBroker.codingEnvironment` checks each item as it checks `secrets` (a name may repeat), opens it, and answers `{schema: "qorpera.coding-environment/v1", environment, mask}`: the distinct values, none the environment holds, newest first. One bad item refuses the whole answer with the same opaque error. An answer without `maskSecrets` (a database before the migration) has an empty `mask`; a broker before this one refuses an answer with it, so the migration goes out with this runtime or after. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-coding-environment.test.ts`, `test/cloud-coding-environment-client.test.ts` and `test/cloud-secret-broker-http.test.ts`.
- The secret broker relays a running coding session's MCP requests and its git fetch, so an MCP sign-in and the GitHub token never leave the broker process (F3 step 5, slice 5j-6b; no SQL: 5j-6a's migration `20260928559210` has the functions; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-STEP-7-CLOUD-CONNECTORS.md`, `deploy/coding-worker/README.md`; 5j-6c's relay unit calls it). `/v1/coding-mcp` (GET, POST, DELETE), `/v1/coding-repository-fetch/refs` (GET) and `/v1/coding-repository-fetch/upload-pack` (POST) open only to the coding executor's own bearer, as `/v1/coding-environment` does, and take no query but `?company=`. The session is named in `x-qorpera-coding-worker`, `x-qorpera-coding-lease` and `x-qorpera-coding-executor`. An MCP request names its server in `x-qorpera-mcp-url` (`https:`, no userinfo or fragment), whose lower-cased origin the kernel is asked for (`qorpera_authorize_coding_mcp_sign_in`, through the new optional `authorizeCodingMcpSignIn`); a fetch names its repository in `x-qorpera-coding-repository`, which must be the session's, compared without case (`qorpera_authorize_coding_repository_fetch`, through `authorizeCodingRepositoryFetch`). Only `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerAuthority` has the two, and a broker without them refuses. The request's shape is checked before the one database read, and the envelope is opened after it. MCP forwards only `content-type`, `accept`, `mcp-session-id`, `mcp-protocol-version` and `last-event-id`, adds the sign-in's `headerName: headerPrefix + value` (API keys only; OAuth is 5j-7), and passes back the status, `content-type` and `mcp-session-id` with `Cache-Control: no-store`; a redirect, a compressed answer or `signIn: null` is the opaque refusal (HTTP 403 `unavailable`). Git goes to `https://github.com/<owner>/<name>.git`, upload-pack only, with `authorization: Basic` for `x-access-token` and the token; it forwards only `content-type` (exactly the upload-pack request's), `content-encoding` (gzip), `accept` and `git-protocol` (`version=N`), and a 200 must carry the upload-pack content type. `openCloudSecretRelay` (new `cloud-secret-broker-relay.ts`) resolves the host once, refuses unless every address is public IPv4 (`nativeEgressPublicAddress`; `resolveNativeHost` is now exported), and dials the first with TLS verified against the host name; the transport is injectable (`relayTransport`). Bounds are per connection only: 10 s to connect, 30 s to the answer's headers, then 5 minutes idle for MCP and 2 for git, on either side. Either side's abort ends both, and bodies stream both ways. A capture for connector `mcp` whose fields are not exactly `{value}`, visible ASCII with inner spaces and at most 4 KiB, is refused before it is sealed. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-secret-broker-relay.test.ts`.
- What the company folder did with a desktop's changes is shown once on that desktop, in its notification area ("Company folder"), whoever holds it (F3 4f-2b-3; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md`, "Notifications"). `CompanyComputerDesktopFolders.listen` tells `(screenId, text)`: each page of a view's commits it reads ("2 changes saved to the company folder.", waiting for a member's approval, not saved because the folder had a newer version, not saved), each refusal in the folder service's notices in the report's words (one told is told again, the same reason and paths, only after a minute, whatever was told between), and a drain's refusal. A run's views are read when its report is and at least every 5 s besides. While the report holds 64 items not shown, those reads only tell the desktop, from where they told it last, and keep nothing; once the report has room it reads the same again from its own place, and the desktop is told only what is new. A run's saves in a row are one report item; a member's view is read the same way, by a record of its own no run is shown, until it is read to its end. The office guest keeps a screen's last 4 notices for 2 minutes and leaves them, oldest first, once the desktop is ready, through the launcher's `notify` (`leaveCompanyComputerDesktopNotice`): a `folder-notice.<n>.<uuid>` file in the desktop's session directory (`/run/qorpera` inside), written whole under a fresh hidden name (0600, `wx`) and renamed, at most 512 bytes of plain text without control characters (a newline aside) or `<`, `>`, `&`, and only the last 4 kept. `qorpera-screen-xfce` watches the directory from inside the desktop's D-Bus session and shows each with `notify-send`, then removes it; with no `notify-send` (#1225's `libnotify-bin`) nothing is shown. Needs a new office image generation.
- A run's report has the change sets the folder service refused whole at a boundary no machine asked for (F3 4f-2b-2b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md`, "Refusals come from the folder service"). Such a refusal makes no commit, so it never reached the run once the desktop action had returned. The folder helper has a new `notices` operation (`{ op, view, after, nonce }`): it runs the folder program's `relay` on `relay/relay.sock` under its root (mode 0700; started on the first ask and again, at most once a second, after it exited) and asks the node's folder service's `notices` role (4f-2b-2a, protocol 4) through it with the view's id and pass, answering only the service's page (`isCompanyComputerFolderNotices`: `{ nonce, notices, last, more }`, never the pass) or `unanswered` (no relay, no answer in 10 s, an error frame such as an older service's "Bad role", a page that is not one). `CompanyComputerDesktopFolders` asks it for each view it opened for a run when it reads the run's report (one ask of a view at a time, 15 s at most), once more when a switch moved the mount on or the view closed (a drain, a release), and, once a view's commits are read to their end, until the service no longer keeps the view (`nonce: null`) or for a minute and a half. A refused change set is the report's refusal ("the company folder refused 3 changes on this desktop"; a paused company's "did not take"), with its paths not saved; the same refusal again before a report gave the run the first is kept once, in its place, with how many times ("(2 times)"), and one after that comes as new, with its paths (the run may have been shown the first already). Needs a new office image generation: the helper is the image's.
- A run's tool results show what the company folder did with its desktop's changes (F3 4f-2b-1; design `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md`, "How 4f-2 carries the report"). The office service's desktop folder (`CompanyComputerDesktopFolders`) keeps a record per run while the run lives (`follow`), with the views it opened for the run and the refusals of the run's drains (paused, closed, unreadable, unproven), and `report` reads each view's commits since the last one shown through `qorpera_folder_view_commits` (4f-2a), at most once a second, and packs them into 2 KiB: the paths saved, at most 20 it did not take with why, one refusal, and `more`. Its mark names what it showed. The native gateway's new `folder_report` action asks the run's session for it under the holder's credential, the native client's `folderReport` asks with the last lease, and `CloudNativeToolBoundary` takes `folderReports`: the result of every call that runs, a refused one's too, carries `companyFolder` before its content (at most 4 KiB, asked for a second at most), and the next ask names its mark once the call answered with it. The runtime instructions say what `companyFolder` means.
- Summary trees, M3 and M3b: erasing one message, and the 7-day prune of replaced tree versions (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10; migrations `20260930441000` and `20260930442000`, SQL tests 960–965; the object gateway's memory message eraser drives the steps). An owner or admin asks with `qorpera_memory_message_erase_request` (a second request for the event joins the first) and reads it with `qorpera_memory_message_erase_status` (`qorpera.memory-message-erase/v1`); `qorpera_memory_message_erase_due` is the eraser's queue, longest waiting first; it passes over the erases the eraser names as resting and any a step holds, and `qorpera_memory_message_erase_take`, the eraser's call before an erase's first step in a pass, moves that erase behind every other erase due (migration `20260930443000`, SQL test 967), so ones whose steps keep failing hide none behind them, and one a pass never reached comes first next time. Step 1 (`_open`) is one memory commit, attributed to the member: under the owner's index row and the conversation's session row it blanks the event and its input to `{"erased": true}` (the hash is kept), raises the conversation's `memory_generation`, deletes every live block holding a line built from the event (at most two per tier, found from the lines' addresses) and the message's images, keeps the other lines of each, records the blocks under the conversation's earlier roots (found by the 1,024 `tree.toml` manifests its id allows) and the runs whose read receipts cover the message. A member who is no longer owner or admin leaves the row `refused`. Step 2 (`_plan`, then `_rewrite` with the plan's hash) writes each deleted block again with its kept lines, merged by position with live lines a later run wrote first, under the owner's current root; a retired owner or a later erase moves it on with nothing written. Step 3 (`_ask`) asks P18's block-history erasure for each block, cut off at step 1's delete, and keeps the copies P18 names. Step 4 (`_scrub`) gives each ended run with no open card P18's receipt scrub; W1's step scrub comes with W1d. A parent entry id, a context source and a history line show an erased body as `(erased)`. M3b: `qorpera_memory_tree_prune`, every minute through pg_cron, deletes a version of a path under a tree root once a later write took it as its prior at least 7 days ago, unless a version or conflict copy outside every tree root, in any folder of the company, holds its manifest (`qorpera_memory_tree_version_held`); such a version waits in `qorpera_memory_tree_prune_held` and is pruned once the copy is erased by its path. Its bytes go through `qorpera_folder_object_release`, the bytes step of P18's phase 5, now shared with the erasure step. A company being deleted is passed over.

- The server name check of "only listed" on a node's agent exit (agent exit E8c-2; no SQL, no runtime code; it needs the kernel modules `nft_queue` and `nfnetlink_queue`, which the nodes get only once Jonas says yes, and without them "only listed" stays E8c-1's check by address; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-EXIT.md` §10 and §12; `deploy/agent-exit/README.md` "The server name check (E8c-2)"). `only_<port>` marks each port 443 flow it lets on (`ct mark 1`), and the base chain `sni` (`qorpera-agent-exit-sni.nft`) queues that flow's packets to queue 0, with no bypass. The checker `qorpera-agent-exit-sni.py` (root in `qexit`, `qorpera-agent-exit-sni.service`) holds the machine's first data until it is a whole ClientHello (at most 16 KiB, at most 5 seconds, at most 64 packets; a hello in several packets is read whole; a packet with no new byte is dropped, not held, a flow has its buffers only while it holds a packet, and one machine's flows hold at most 512 packets) and lets it on only when it names one server the company's rules allow and block none over; the flow is then marked 2 and queued no more. So that one machine cannot fill the node's one queue of 8192, a machine's packets of flows being judged go to it at 1000 a second at most and 250 at once (the rest are dropped before it, in `dropped_sni_rate`), and a flow is refused past 128 packets, 10 seconds without a whole hello, or 5 seconds after its pass without the server's acknowledgement (some 30 machines sending at the same moment can still fill it: the kernel then drops, failing closed, and TCP sends again; the checker's status counts such drops and its journal names them, and `verify` turns unhealthy only when they come in 3 status writes in a row, for a minute after). Anything else is refused: the flow is marked 3 and queued no more, the machine reset, and the refusal counted in `refused_name`, and the logger names it `name_not_listed`. With no checker the kernel drops what is queued, so nothing passes unread; systemd starts the checker again a second after any stop, with no limit (its unit has `MemoryMax=2G`), and a new checker refuses flows whose start it did not see, at their next packet either way. It writes `sni.json` every 2 seconds; `up` and the reload load the chain and start the unit only while both modules are loaded, `check` says which way "only listed" runs, and `verify` names a missing chain or checker and the checker's problems, lasting drops of the queue among them. Limits: names only (no domain fronting check; ECH stays off by E6's browser policy), and a hello sent with pauses longer than the machine's retransmission time must fit one segment. ROLLOUT.md step 11 brings the code to a node that runs E8c-1 without the modules, and step 12 loads them. Proven in Docker (the checker's judge against crafted hellos) and in an LXD VM, with step 11 and step 12 from E8c-1.
- The coding guest agent gives a session's folder program a local folder on the machine's disk (F3 step 5g-4; `--local-dir <state>/sessions/<worker>/folder-local`, the folder program's from 5d on): the session's own git files and its cache folders. It outlives a new view, as the program keeps what is still the session's, and goes when the session stops.
- Added `cloud-coding-guest-agent`, the agent inside a member's coding machine (F3 step 5g-2b; nothing runs it yet). The executor pushes it to `/opt/qorpera-guest/agent.mjs` and runs one verb per LXD exec as root: one JSON request on stdin, one answer line on stdout (contracts' `cloud-coding-guest`).
- The coding guest agent answers `quiesce` (F3 step 5g-6a; `quiesceCodingGuestSession`): it stops the session's slice and its user manager, which lingers no more, keeps its view mounted, and refuses with `CODING_GUEST_SESSION_STILL_RUNNING` while either still runs (`CODING_GUEST_SESSION_BUSY` while another verb holds the session). Its `status` observes every command with no outcome, however old, lists those still running before the newest of the rest, and refuses with `CODING_GUEST_STATUS_OVERFLOW` when more run than one answer holds.
  - `mount` gives a session its Linux user `qorpera-<uid>` in group `qorpera-coding` (passwd files, never deleted, explicit 65536-wide sub-id ranges), its own network namespace behind the machine's NAT (nft inside the guest only), its folder view at `/run/qorpera-folder/mounts/<worker32>` (the folder must sit in memory), and a user manager that sees the view at `/company`.
  - The view counts as mounted once the folder program's own mount (source `qorpera-company`, type `fuse` as the program mounts directly as root, or `fuse.qorpera` through fusermount) is the top one at the session's mountpoint.
  - `run` starts each command once, as the session's user, in its own transient unit through `agent.mjs relay`. The relay writes the output into a bounded ring of files and the exit into `exit.json`. The machine's disk keeps each command's intent and outcome. The command's environment passes only through a root-only file in `/run/qorpera-guest/inputs`. systemd hands that file to the relay as stdin, and the agent removes it once the unit has started.
  - `output` reads from a byte offset, naming a gap once the ring dropped bytes, and can wait for new bytes.
  - `cancel`, `status` and `stop-session` complete the verbs. `stop-session` keeps the user and the NAT.
  - Every refusal is an error code that never quotes a value.
- A company run writes its own conversation's summary tree (M1b-4b-3; design `RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.3, §5.4, §7.1). `CloudConversationLines` (`memory-tree/conversation-lines.ts`, for any conversation kind) reads the conversation and its tree before the catalog is admitted, so a run with a view gets zoom; it builds lines with `memoryCompact` through the run's compactor and stores them through `CloudCompanyFolderClient.memorySessionCommitStaged` at the generation it read: the first batch makes the root, `tree.toml` and the tiers, blocks go in change sets within 64 files and 1 MiB, a stale generation discards the batch whole, a block that changed is read again and merged up to 3 times, and each batch records one `memory_compaction`. `runCloudCodexToolSession` takes an `opening` hook, run after the admission and before the agent's thread, whose result is the agent's input, instructions and composer; its `compactor` is answered from the opening, and `closing` runs after the agent's turn. At the opening the run settles `[0, T)` within `SETTLE_MS` and records its wait in `memory_view` (`waitMs`, `waitedLines`); after its turn it closes the level-0 lines of the events it caused, and every merge they complete, within `CLOSE_MS`. Live compactor calls are not yet tested against a provider.
- Agents meet a company folder's links and never follow one (folder links LK2; LK1's migration `20260930380000`, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-LINKS.md`). `CloudCompanyFolderClient` parses an entry of kind `symlink` with its `target` (1 to 4,095 bytes; a target on any other kind is refused) and an agent proposal's new-folder items by kind, a link with its target; `readText` of a link throws `CloudCompanyPathNotText` ("Company folder path is a link"). `company_read` of a link answers `{path, kind: "symlink", version, target}`, released as a file's read is, and a path through a link reads absent. `company_list` gives each link its `target`, and listing a link refuses with `COMPANY_FILE_NOT_A_FOLDER`. `company_write` at a link's version writes nothing and takes no receipt: it answers `not_applied` with reason `link` and the link's target, released. `company_mkdir` at a link's path is a conflict (`exists`); `company_delete` deletes the link only. Notebooks, skills and interfaces never follow one: a notebook path that is a link is not text and a notebook listing leaves links out; a skill whose `SKILL.md` is a link is named `unreadable` in the catalog, so `update_skill` never writes over it; an interface's file that is a link is not served, and an interface write refuses a link as not a folder or not a file. An agent's proposal review lists each link a new folder holds with its target.
- A coding session's turns are runs of their own, in SQL (agent memory tree W6a, after W1b-1; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §10.1; migration `20260930250000`, SQL tests 921 and 979). Each turn keeps the wake occurrence its begin made, and its report names its run (`runId`). A turn is narrow once its session's view holds a path outside the every-agent part: its begin and its end mark it when the session's coding view has read one, and F3's view refresh calls `qorpera_coding_turn_mark_narrow` while it is open; a session is narrow while any turn or its view is, or any turn's run is narrow by its place or its log (`qorpera_coding_session_narrow`). The run log's machine check, `qorpera_memory_run_machine_narrow` (W1b-1's, false until now), is true for a turn's run when that turn or an earlier one is narrow, or, while the turn is open, when the view is, so the run's `run.toml` and every block written from then on are narrow (W1b-1's file rules ask it at each block write, and `qorpera_memory_step_narrow` answers it as `machine`). A narrow session's busy check-in names it only as `a coding session that read files only some agents may read` and each running command by its `commandId` and turn alone: the wake bus admits that shape for a coding session only, and the check-in's prompt says so. P18 notes a session's turn runs wherever it notes the session: a version or copy its view's commit wrote, a change set of its view, a read record of its view, and each noted turn run. The runtime's side (coding logs, the narrow check before each batch) is W6b.
- The office image has a dependency recipe and a candidate builder (F3 step 4, slice 4e-1; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Slices"). `deploy/lxd-company-computer-guest/office-image-dependencies.json` pins the Ubuntu base, the apt packages (fuse3 and the 2026-10-14 toolbox among them), Node.js 24, Chrome for Testing, uv, mise, `typescript` and `tsx`, and lists the toolbox checks. `install-office-image-dependencies.sh` installs it offline in the guest being built, with one `/usr/bin/node`, the self-update units masked and swap off for good. `scripts/build-office-image-candidate.sh` builds a candidate on node01's test machine only, in a claimed nested project with no network card (its apt reads only its own configuration, so none of the test machine's apt hooks run), and runs every toolbox check from a computer's shell as the office account under the installed office unit's confinement (`test/fixtures/company-computer-linux-toolbox-probe.ts`); an AppArmor `file_inherit` denial while it runs fails the build, and the evidence records `fusermount3` as root (its AppArmor mode and its output through Node's stdio and through a pipe). `fusermount3` is checked as root only: FUSE is root's, so it is not in the toolbox. It deletes the candidate unless `--stage` keeps it on that test machine for the folder remount proof (`deploy/test-machine/OFFICE-IMAGE-CANDIDATE.md`). A computer's shell and desktops see `/etc/libreoffice` read-only when the image has it: LibreOffice's registry links there, and without it LibreOffice cannot start; the office folder remount proof's shapes and pins follow. The folder program, the helper and their units follow in 4e-2.
- The office image carries the company folder (F3 step 4, slice 4e-2; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Slices"). `scripts/package-company-computer.mjs --folder-program <built qorpera-folder>` packages the folder program (only the build `deploy/folder-service/folder-program-linux-x64.json` pins), the 4d-1 helper and their units. The office image installer admits the five files all together (`OFFICE_IMAGE_FOLDER_PACKAGE_FILES`, else `OFFICE_IMAGE_PACKAGE_INCOMPLETE`), installs the program at `/opt/qorpera-folder/qorpera-folder`, renders `qorpera-office-folder-mount@.service` with the office account's ids and keeps the folder's units stopped and unmodified like the office units. The office service wants and starts after the helper's socket. The helper stays out of the code package (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_SERVICE_SOFTWARE_FILES`): a code package never changes the folder's files, and `verifyLxdOfficeCodePackageProvision` renders a folder base's mount template from `baseFolderMountTemplate` (lane document `baseFolderMountTemplatePath`, staged as `base-folder-mount-template`; `scripts/prepare-office-code-package-provision.mjs --folder-mount-template`) and refuses it unless the base carries exactly those bytes. `scripts/build-office-image-candidate.sh` builds the program and checks the folder's units and program in the candidate (`deploy/test-machine/OFFICE-IMAGE-CANDIDATE.md`).
- A run can write its own conversation's summary tree through a checked memory commit (M1b-4b-2; migration `20260930300000`, SQL test 947). With no `runLog`, its units may only make that tree's root or tiers and write `tree.toml` or blocks, and `sessionGenerations` must name exactly its own conversation; a stale generation discards the batch. As in its run log, the run writes there as itself: neither the member it works for nor its agent needs a folder grant. `qorpera_memory_events_run` proves the binding before reading at most 128 raw included events of that conversation, preserving each event's sequence and hash, and names the conversation's tree root, where the writer makes the tree before it is indexed. `qorpera_memory_compaction_record` stores counts and the existing stop reason once per batch and assignment, derives the payer from the run's pinned sponsor, replays an identical record and refuses changed content. Both calls have distinct binding-scoped native host names. The runtime tree writer, settle and closing remain separate work; no company is enabled here.
- The office image has a production build, a cold-boot acceptance and a provenance signer (F3 step 4, slice 4e-3; no SQL, no runtime code; `deploy/first-node/OFFICE-IMAGE-PRODUCTION.md`; merge after #1110). The recipe both builders share moves unchanged into `scripts/office-image-recipe.sh`. `scripts/build-office-image-production.sh` runs as root on node01's host from a clean checkout: it builds from the base the recipe pins, by full fingerprint in the host LXD (copied into the project only when missing), in a VM with no profile and no network card in the given project and pool (`qofleet01`, `qorpera-vm`, an 80 GiB root, at most the 1 TiB a guest takes), only with 16 GiB available, gives the office account the workbench uid/gid of the physical policy it will be pinned into (`--policy`; one id, and the host's workbench must be that id's NFS mount, `0700`, so preparation's id check holds), takes the folder program prebuilt and only as the pinned build, publishes the image with A1's labels and no alias, and deletes only its VM and its base copy. `scripts/accept-office-image.sh` boots a copy once in a throwaway project and proves from inside the VM KVM and Secure Boot, the office account (the policy's id), the installation receipt and package manifest the labels name, the office units never started, empty credentials, no JWT- or Supabase-secret-shaped string on the disk, the unconfigured services refused and Chromium's sandbox (A1's verifier). `scripts/sign-office-image-provenance.mjs` makes the ed25519 key once as root (`--generate-key`) and, with `sign`, holds the two evidence files and that policy (parsed as the orchestrator does: its workbench ids, architecture and root) against each other, signs the statement `verifyLxdOfficeImageProvenance` checks, and writes the image fields of a physical policy and the policy with them, checked with `assertLxdOfficePhysicalImage`, printing its `providerPolicySha256`. Nothing of it runs in production before P2's proof and v179.
- The office image candidate takes the office account's id (F3 step 4, a 4e follow-up; no SQL, no runtime code; `deploy/test-machine/OFFICE-IMAGE-CANDIDATE.md`; merge after #1180). `scripts/build-office-image-candidate.sh --office-id <N>` (1 to 2147483647; by default 20048, the workbench account of `qofleet01`'s policy) gives the office account that uid and gid, as the production build does from `--policy`. A node prepares an office only as its policy's workbench account, so a candidate with A1's 20045 proved an account no node of this fleet prepares.
- The office image can show desktop notices (F3 step 4, for 4f-2; no SQL, no runtime code; the image generation after v180). `office-image-dependencies.json`'s `office` group adds `libnotify-bin` (`notify-send`) and `xfce4-notifyd`, the desktop's notification daemon.
- Dormant resumed-run composition (memory W5b-2) opens only a live, non-narrow log at its discovered next step, validates contiguous historical blocks through the shared parser, and keeps erased steps as `(erased)` lines while listing them as unknown outcomes, since their results are gone (§12). The binding-scoped resume adapter records every pending call, every erased step and every unrelated unknown receipt before returning the log, the step lines and a composer. The run's work messages must come from that returned `composeContext`: the step lines' own `composeContext()` leaves the warnings out. Explicit warnings survive summary folding and carry exactly the recorded identifiers, with no guessed receipt-to-call link. Missing, changed, erasing or malformed logs refuse opening; this component does not activate production logging or resumption. The HTTP control client reads `qorpera_memory_run_resume_record`'s 23505 (a different record for a run and assignment that already stored one) as a refusal, `CloudControlRpcRefused` with that code, so the run's retry stops at once instead of repeating the refused request; 23505 from any other call is read as before.
- Worker memory reads prove the current run before selecting their scope (agent memory tree W4b; migration `20260930341000`, SQL test 997). `qorpera_folder_worker_parent_run(p_binding)` derives the worker actor and environment after the run check and delegates to the existing parent API. `qorpera_memory_view_reference_check_run(p_binding, p_view)` derives the company after the same check and preserves the reference outcomes `ok`, `generation_moved`, `block_gone` and `erasure_covers`. The binding wrappers have distinct names so native dispatch never selects between overloaded functions; the existing APIs remain available to their callers. This adds SQL and host dispatch only, without worker composition, runtime activation or payer changes.
- A resumed run records what it recovered from its log (agent memory tree W5b-2; migration `20260930340000`, SQL test 996; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §12, §13). Two binding-scoped native host calls serve the current assignment: `qorpera_memory_run_resume` returns null for an original attempt, or the event-derived reason (`restart` or `limit_reset`) and its unknown receipts' ids and operation keys; `qorpera_memory_run_resume_record` records `stepsRead` and every log call and receipt shown as outcome unknown: a pending call, an erased step (its result is gone, §12) and an `outcome_unknown` receipt. A log call names its step and provider call id; a receipt with no known log link names only its receipt id, never an inferred call. SQL derives the reason and enriches receipt entries from the run's own ledger. The record is written once per run and assignment: an exact replay returns it, and changed replay content is refused with 23505. Both calls prove the live run binding before reporting malformed input (22023). This adds SQL and native host dispatch only; the runtime's resume composer and lifecycle activation remain separate work, and no production run starts writing logs here.
- `CloudRunLogCompanyFolder` persists a run's log through the ordinary run-actor folder client (memory W1c-3): trusted location/origin lookup, bounded verified reads, normal upload staging and structured parses, and `memoryCommitStaged` with `runLog` and tree-only `runGenerations`. `CloudRunLog` takes the fresh platform origin before creating the log and retries the exact origin-change or machine-narrow refusal once; a machine retry keeps immutable lines and keyed argument hashes and narrows changed lines before staging again. Native host calls match the W1b SQL registrations. No production lifecycle constructs a log yet; matching SQL, closing/compaction and erasure support remain activation gates.
- A coding turn's context takes the stop reason `MACHINE_STOPPED` (F3 5g-1b; migration `20260930431000`): the session's machine stopped from outside, such as a node restart, and ended what its commands ran. The coding instructions tell the agent to check what those commands left before relying on it. A runtime without it refuses such a context (`CODING_TURN_CONTEXT_INVALID`), so it goes out before or with the executor that records it.
- A coding turn's context takes the stop reason `FOLDER_RESTART` (F3 5g-5 part 2; migration `20260930501000`): the executor restarted the session's machine because the company folder could not confirm what the machine had read, and that ended what its commands ran. The coding instructions tell the agent to check those commands as after `MACHINE_STOPPED`. A runtime without it refuses such a context (`CODING_TURN_CONTEXT_INVALID`), so it goes out before the migration and the executor that record it.
- `coding_run_command` and `coding_read_command` tell the model about a coding command's `machine_stopped` state (the machine stopped under it; it may have done part of its work, and it is not success) and `log.gapBytes` (output lost before it was saved, not in the log) (F3 step 5g-3a). Nothing sends either yet.
- Summary trees M3, the copies in a member's answer (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10; migration `20260930470000`, SQL test 680): `qorpera_memory_message_erase_request` and `_status` answer `copies`, `[{environmentId, path, live}]`, the copies P18 named for the blocks of every erase of the message at step 3 (files at paths no tree owns, in any folder-v2 environment of the company, that hold a version's bytes, which the erase leaves), each environment and path once and in byte order, `live` when the path is in that folder now (else only its history holds the copy), empty before step 3. A duplicate request's erase, which ends done with no blocks, names the first erase's copies. A member erases such a copy by its environment and path. The steps' answers, which only the eraser reads, are unchanged.
- Run logs in the company folder (agent memory tree W1b-1; migration `20260930231000`, SQL tests 951 and 952; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §7, §8). A run's log is `runs/<run>/` under its conversation's tree root: `run.toml`, `log/<8 digits>.jsonl` (16 steps a block) and the run's own tree `t<n>/<8 digits>.jsonl`, indexed in `qorpera_memory_run_logs` and `qorpera_memory_run_steps` (with the folder keys each step names, for erasure). Each plain step line carries `paths`, the paths the step named in its arguments and receipt, not its reads (at most 1,024, 8 KiB as stored; a line is at most 49 KiB), and its needles are those paths and the paths its arguments, result, reads and JSON output name. A memory commit's request names its log (`runLog`) and its run's generation (`runGenerations`); a run writes only its own log, steps only grow (a finished line names every path its pending line named, unless it is narrow), and a narrow log takes only narrow lines. The native host gets four calls: `qorpera_memory_run_log_place` (where the log is or goes), `qorpera_memory_step_hashes` (a narrow step's keyed hashes) and `qorpera_memory_step_narrow` (what makes the next lines narrow: the log, the machine and the paths not every agent reads), both under the run's lease, and `qorpera_folder_memory_commit` as the run. The runtime's writer is W1c-3.
- Agents open Stripe hooks on folder-v2, and the Company Computer reads a Stripe hook's signing secret off Stripe's page into the vault (folder core C4, E3c-2; no SQL: E3c-1's migration `20260930344000` has the functions; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5.8; it ships in one release with E3c-3's office image and E3c-4's migration `20260930345000`). `manage_hook`'s `create` takes `proof: stripe`, whose site is always `dashboard.stripe.com`, named or not (any other is refused: "A Stripe hook's secret is taken from dashboard.stripe.com"), and answers with `signingSecret.ready: false`. Its description says to open one hook per Stripe endpoint, test mode apart from live mode, and how to read the secret; `list_hooks`'s says what a Stripe hook's `version`, `capturedAt` and `lastRefusal` mean. The secret broker opens a Stripe hook with a capture key instead of a secret it makes: `CloudSecretKeyring.capturePublicKey` derives the hook's X25519 key from the company's key (HKDF-SHA256, `qorpera.event-hook-capture/v1`), and `openCapture` opens a box sealed to it (`@qorpera/runtime/cloud-event-hook-capture-box`, `qorpera.event-hook-capture-box/v1`, bound to the company, the computer and its generation, the receipt, the hook and the field, for 90 s). `/v1/event-hooks` also takes `{action: rekey, hook}`, which replaces a hook's capture key after a key renewal while nothing was taken under the current one. A new route, `POST /v1/event-hook-captures` (`qorpera.event-hook-capture-request/v1`, the run's lease in `x-qorpera-identity` proven first, as on `/v1/event-hook-fills`), reads the capture's context, opens the box against the database's facts only, checks the secret's shape (`whsec_` and 16 to 200 base64 characters), and stores it sealed as the hook's next version, or answers `unchanged` when it is the current one; when another capture of the hook stored first (40003), it reads again, up to 3 times, for the same receipt and box. `computer_browser` takes `{action: vault_capture, role: button | link, name, hook, field: signing_secret}`: the native host asks for the rekey first, has the office read the secret off the page into a box, and relays the box to the broker; the box is never in the answer, the log or the receipt. The review card reads "Read a hook's signing secret off a web page into the vault", and a Stripe hook's `create` card names Stripe as the signer and `dashboard.stripe.com` as the one site its secret is read from. Every answer the native tool boundary returns has `whsec_` secrets cut out (`whsec_[REDACTED]`), its digest and run log included.
- The Company Computer's office reads a Stripe hook's signing secret off Stripe's page for a `vault_capture` (folder core C4, E3c-3, the office image; no SQL: E3c-1's migration `20260930344000` has `qorpera_native_office_hook_capture`; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5.8 point 2; it ships in one release with E3c-2 and E3c-4). `CloudCompanyComputerEffectControl.hookCaptureTarget` reads where the box goes from the database itself, for a begun, live receipt of this call only: the hook, its address and site, and its capture key (the HTTP registry's closed method list takes `qorpera_native_office_hook_capture`). `captureCompanyHookSecret` (`@qorpera/runtime/cloud-company-computer-hook-capture`) refuses a page off the hook's site, one that does not show the hook's address, a control in a frame, and a page that shows a secret before the click (after one reload); it covers the whole page (the reload too), clicks the named control, and takes the one `whsec_` value shown alone, twice the same 300 ms apart and whole; whether it took it or not, it clears the clipboard and reloads the page so Stripe masks the value again, and uncovers only a page that no longer shows it (the page is also read as the browser draws it, its open shadow roots and slots in place, and a value split across nodes reads whole where only inline boxes come between its pieces or where one starts where the other ends, on its line; text under `display: none` or `visibility: hidden` is not read). A human check the click brought up is read before that reload, recorded as the site's stop, and answered as the stop, with no box. The guest seals the value to the hook's capture key (`sealEventHookCapture`) and holds the box for this call's answer only, once the page shows no check (a stop answer has none); `CompanyComputerHookCaptureBoxes` keeps at most four, each for 90 s, per session. The native gateway sends the box beside the answer's content, and refuses a done read whose box is gone (`VAULT_CAPTURE_LOST`). An office that cannot read the target fails with `VAULT_CAPTURE_UNAVAILABLE` before any click (the database's reason as its cause); a refused read fails with its reason (`VAULT_CAPTURE_WRONG_SITE`, `_IN_FRAME`, `_WRONG_HOOK`, `_AMBIGUOUS`, `_NOT_SHOWN`, `_UNSTABLE`, `_PARTIAL`), any other error as `VAULT_CAPTURE_FAILED` without the page's words, and a page that still shows the value after the reload has its tab closed (`VAULT_CAPTURE_NOT_HIDDEN`), as `ACCOUNT_FILL_LOST` does. Every value read is hidden from the agent from then on, and text shaped like a signing secret (`whsec_`, also percent-encoded once or more, hidden whole, before any recorded value that is a part of one) is hidden from every snapshot, title, address and tab name, and no screenshot is taken while a page shows one, whoever put it there (that check reads the page as the browser draws it too, so a value split across nodes or shadow roots is seen whole).
- The secret broker gives a coding session its environment (F3 step 5, slice 5j-3; no SQL: 5j-1's migration `20260930438000` has the function; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-STEP-2-SECRET-CUSTODY.md`, `deploy/coding-worker/README.md`; the executor calls it in a later slice). `POST /v1/coding-environment` takes `{schema: "qorpera.coding-environment-request/v1", workerId, leaseToken, executorOwner}` and opens only to the coding executor's own bearer, as `/v1/repository-credentials` does: the runtime token never opens it, and a broker without `codingExecutorToken` answers 404. `CloudSecretBroker.codingEnvironment` asks the kernel (`qorpera_authorize_coding_environment`, through the new optional `authorizeCodingEnvironment` that only `PostgresCloudSecretBrokerAuthority` has, since `service_role` cannot run it), checks the answer exactly (the session's worker and repository, upper-case names that never repeat, each envelope's digest), opens the envelopes one at a time, and answers `{schema: "qorpera.coding-environment/v1", environment}`, by name in the kernel's order, with `Cache-Control: no-store`. Every refusal is the same opaque error (HTTP 403 `unavailable`), and one bad secret refuses the whole answer. A value is exactly one field, `value`, of at least `CODING_ENVIRONMENT_VALUE_MINIMUM_BYTES` (8) UTF-8 bytes and without a NUL, the length the coding machine masks (`CODING_SECRET_MASK_MINIMUM_BYTES`, 5j-2); a capture for connector `environment` that is not so is refused before it is sealed. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-coding-environment.test.ts` and `test/cloud-secret-broker-http.test.ts`.
- "Only listed" by address on a node's agent exit (agent exit E8c-1; no SQL, no runtime code, no new kernel modules; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-EXIT.md` §10; `deploy/agent-exit/README.md` "Where a company's machines may go"). An "only listed" company's resolver answers only its listed names that are not blocked (every other name is "no such name") and puts each address it gives out in the company's nft set `allow_<port>`. The exit's map `reach` sends each verified machine to the chain `open` (open mode, or no rules) or to its company's `only_<port>`, which lets TCP 443 to the set's addresses through and refuses the rest as `not_listed`; a machine without an entry is refused as `rules_unknown` (and has its connections ended when its entry goes), and the logger names both from the mark each refusal keeps in the refused-flow sets. A switch to or from "only listed", or a narrower list, restarts the company's resolver (about a second without names; a marker `names/<port>.owed` carries it over a crash) and, unless the company is now open, ends its machines' connections once `reach` is right; a wider list stays a reload. The exit's reload empties the sets and the reconciler reloads those resolvers; `up` fills `reach` and the companies' sets and chains from `names.py elements`; the plug's forget drops a machine's `reach` entry. ROLLOUT.md step 10 adds it to a node that runs E8b-2; step 9 brings it to one that runs E8b. Proven in Docker and in an LXD VM, with ROLLOUT.md step 9 and step 10 from previous releases. E8c-2 (the TLS server name check) needs `nfnetlink_queue` and `nft_queue` on the nodes.
- The office has a folder helper that mounts the company folder for each desktop and command (F3 step 4, slice 4d-1; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Slices"; the office service uses it from 4d-2 and the office image carries it from 4e). `qorpera-office-folder-helper.service` (`cloud-company-computer-folder-helper.ts`, entry `cloud-company-computer-folder-helper-main.ts`) runs as root in PID 1's mount namespace and answers one JSON line per connection on its socket, `/run/qorpera-folder-helper/helper.sock`, which only the office account opens (`cloud-company-computer-folder-helper-client.ts`). `mount` writes the view to `/run/qorpera-folder/views/<name>.json`, makes an empty state folder and a mountpoint inside a parent bound onto itself and shared, and starts the folder program as its own unit (`qorpera-office-folder-mount@<name>.service`); it answers `mounted` once mountinfo shows the folder there, `closed` when the node closed or refused the view (the program's exit 3), and otherwise gives the name back. `switch` hands a desktop's mount to its next view (same environment, another view id) and answers `switched` once the program's proof file names it (#1018), `pending` until then. `boundary` runs the program's `publish` (`--final` for a command) and answers `saved`, `refused`, `closed` or `unanswered`, with the report it printed. `unmount` publishes first, and stops the unit and removes the mount, view and state folder only once that was saved or the view closed. `drop` gives a name back without publishing, and `list` names the held mounts with their program's state. A name is held exactly while its view file exists, and one name's requests run one at a time. The mountpoint's mode and the mount unit's restarts stay as they are until the remount proof (Q3) settles them.
- Each desktop of the office has the company folder at `/company` (F3 step 4, slice 4d-2a; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "A desktop's mount"). `CompanyComputerDesktopFolders` (`cloud-company-computer-desktop-folder.ts`) keeps one mount per screen, named `desktop-<screenId>`: before a run's action, and when a member takes control, it opens the holder's folder view (`qorpera_folder_view_open`, the platform opener with the run or member as anchor) and asks the folder helper to `mount` it, or to `switch` a running mount to it; the same holder keeps a mount whose program still runs. A mount whose program ended, or that the node closed, is unmounted and made again. The mount goes, and its view closes, when its desktop stops, a minute after a run's open found no desktop, and at the office's start (`drop`, for every mount the helper still holds). A run whose view the core refused, or that has no mount within the action's wait, gets its screen back with nothing done (`completed: false`, `refused` with `COMPANY_FOLDER_REFUSED` and the core's reason, or `COMPANY_FOLDER_UNAVAILABLE`), and only an explicit open asks for it again; a member keeps the desktop. The registry passes a refusal's reason on only for the folder view's known refusals; a core that does not answer, or answers busy (`55P03`), is asked again until the wait ends. The desktop binds the mount's parent at `/run/qorpera-company` with `/company` linking into it, and `QORPERA_COMPANY_FOLDER` names it (Q3, #1007): a run's desktop that runs on an older mount, or on none, starts again on the new one, its shell and commands going on. The service uses the helper only when its socket exists; the office image carries it from 4e.
- A desktop's folder boundaries: a drain and a screenshot (F3 step 4, slice 4d-2b; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Drains and stops" and "Before output reaches a model"). A drain publishes the screen's mount before it is acknowledged: the drain supervisor gives the stop 20 s (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_DRAIN_STOP_MS`) and passes it a deadline a second before that, and a stop that runs out is never acknowledged. After the desktop stopped, `stopResources` asks the mount's boundary (`unmount`, not final); no answer, `invalid`, `unanswered` and a refused `run_ended` are asked again each second until the deadline, every other answer is final, and a final answer that left the mount drops it without another boundary. The drain logs the answer word, the commit and how many paths had each outcome, never a path or a reason. No final answer by the deadline, or a mount that did not go, fails the drain and leaves the mount for the stop control; a desktop started again meanwhile keeps its mount. A desktop screenshot asks the mount's boundary (`boundary`, not final) after the PNG checks and before it is published, due 4 s before the call's reply deadline (`perform` takes it): saved, or refused for some paths, lets it go on; otherwise the result is `{completed: false, heldBack: {code: "COMPANY_FOLDER_HELD_BACK", message}}` with no image, the message saying why (paused, run ended, refused, no answer in time, or restarted). `closed`, `unreadable` and `unproven` take rule 4's way out: the desktop stops first (its browser, then its windows, never the capturing run's session), then the mount drops and its view closes, and the screen's next action starts the desktop again on a new mount. A stop that fails drains the screen (`AUTHORITY_LOST`), and the mount drops once that drain stopped the desktop; a drop that fails leaves no holder the mount, and the screen's next hold drops it first. Until it goes, a refused mount is no screen's `/company` (`mounted()` hides it), and a screenshot of a desktop that stopped or started again since its action began is held back as restarted. The native tool boundary issues no picture for a result with `completed: false`, so 4d-2a's given-back screenshot gets none either.
- Each shell command of the office runs in the company folder at `/company`, offline unless the model asks for the network (F3 step 4, slice 4d-4b; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "A command's mount" and "Before output reaches a model"). With the folder helper, `startCompanyComputerLinuxOfficeService` makes `CompanyComputerCommandFolders` (`cloud-company-computer-command-folder.ts`) and each screen's shell starts its commands through it: a folder view opens for the command's receipt (`computer_shell_command`), the helper mounts it, and the helper's `run` op starts the command in the shell's namespaces, with its own network or the shell's as the start says. A command whose view, mount or run fails ends without running, with why, and its mount drops (asked again from 5 s, doubling up to 5 min) before its view closes; a run with no answer is asked `ended` until the helper knows. A folder command's output reaches only the run that started it, and only up to its folder's last boundary: a read, or the wait after a start or input, asks `boundary` (due a second before the reply, at most 30 s), and saved, or refused for some paths, lets it out. Otherwise the reply holds the output back (`heldBack: {code: "COMPANY_FOLDER_HELD_BACK", message}`: paused, run ended, refused, or no answer in time), and `closed`, `unreadable`, `unproven` and `unknown` stop the command for good. When the command ends, its final boundary (`unmount`, final) is asked again until it has a final answer, with the output held back as saving meanwhile, and its mount drops: saved, the view closed by the final (`closed: true`, some paths perhaps not taken), or a refusal but the run check's or the pause's; a refusal with no reason means the core has not answered yet, or a change still waits. A command a boundary stopped for good while it ended has its mount dropped without a final boundary. A reply lists once the paths the folder did not take (`notSaved`, at most 20 and 8 KiB, `moreNotSaved` counting the rest): those an output read's boundary settled, and every one of the final's or of a boundary that stopped the command (no later boundary reports what it kept). `result`, `list` and the endpoint's `settle` take the run asking; a command another run started shows its state, never its output. A shell's usage counts what runs in its pid namespace, so a folder command (outside the Bubblewrap monitor's tree) counts too. "Folder view command is not current" is a folder view refusal the office passes on. The run's computer tools fill in `network: false` on a start unless the model asks, and their description says so, its shell part shortened to keep it within a tool registration's 4 KiB; the action review lets an offline start, and input to a command this run started offline (`offlineShells`), through without review, and a networked start's card says it has internet access. A start that names its network is refused by an office from before this slice, and an offline one by an office without the folder helper (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_OFFLINE_UNAVAILABLE`), so this runtime ships with the office image that has the helper (4e). Tests in `test/cloud-company-computer-command-folder.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-shell.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-linux-guest.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-tools.test.ts`, `test/cloud-action-review.test.ts` and `test/cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts`.
- A drain waits for the mounts of the screen's shell commands (F3 step 4, slice 4d-4c; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Drains and stops"). The shell's commands end with it, and `stopResources` with a drain's deadline then waits for each command mount of the screen beside the desktop's (`CompanyComputerCommandFolders.drain`; `command` takes the screen id, and the shell's `screenId` option is required with its folders): its final boundary has a final answer and its mount went. Meanwhile the final boundary, the mount's drop and the end check are asked again each second instead of backing off (a wait already running is cut short), the final boundary and the drop within the deadline, and in its last 2 s at their usual pace again; no answer, `invalid`, `unanswered`, and a refusal with no reason or the run check's are asked again unless the final closed the view, and the pause's refusal is final, as at the desktop's drain, so a mount it left drops. Whether a drain waits is judged when the final answer comes, so a final boundary already asked as the drain began counts for it too. The drain logs the final answer as the desktop's does. A command mount still there at the deadline fails the drain (`OFFICE_FOLDER_DRAIN_UNANSWERED`, logged as no final answer or a mount that did not go) and is left for the stop control; its own final boundary goes on as before. A command whose view or mount never came is not waited for, nor is another screen's. Tests in `test/cloud-company-computer-command-folder.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-shell.test.ts` and `test/cloud-company-computer-linux-guest.test.ts`.
- The office's stop publishes the mounts the office service left (F3 step 4, slice 4d-4d; no SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "The office's stop publishes the mounts left"). Once the service is stopped, and before it writes its completion, `controlCompanyComputerServiceStop` asks the folder helper for its mounts and asks each one's boundary at once, a command's as its final one, then drops a mount a final answer left (`publishCompanyComputerFoldersLeft`, `cloud-company-computer-service-stop-folders.ts`). No answer, QRC01, the run check, or a refusal with no reason while a change still waits (a command's final that did not close its view, or a desktop's boundary that kept a path: the core did not answer the commit, or a path is left waiting) fails the run with no completion, and the provider's retry asks again; a run that finds a completion, and an inspection, ask nothing. A desktop's drain (`CompanyComputerDesktopFolders.drain`) now asks such a boundary again too, instead of dropping the mount and its kept changes. The boundaries end 15 s before the run's 100 s (`COMPANY_COMPUTER_SERVICE_STOP_CONTROL_MS`). The folder helper now logs each boundary's report as it answers (`companyComputerFolderAnswerLogged`: the answer, the refusal, the commit and how many paths had each outcome, never a path or a reason; the drain's log uses it too), so a stop's report, which the stop control cannot write, outlives the office service in the helper's journal. An image without the helper has no socket, and the step asks nothing. The stop control ships with the office software.
- A shell command in the company folder shows its output to a later run of the same agent and member once that run took in the command's reads (F3 step 4, slice 4d-4e; migration `20260930491000`, SQL test 430; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "A command that outlived its run"). `qorpera_folder_office_inherit_command_reads(environment, command, receipt)`, service role only, finds the reading run from the office resource of the action that reads (on the command's screen and office, not the command's own start: else `Folder inheritance read is not current`; its run's job leased: else `Run is unavailable`) and runs `qorpera_folder_run_inherit_view_reads` for it with the command's view and the receipt as the inherit id. The endpoint passes the action's receipt to `settle`, and the shell's `result` takes it as `receiptId`. A read by another run that names its action has the command's boundary released first (3 s earlier than its own run's), then `CompanyComputerCommandFolder.inherit` asks the take-in, and output released before it is shown once it answers, with the paths not saved that release reported and none a later boundary reported meanwhile. A take-in that does not answer `inherited`, or a release held back, puts those paths back in front of any reported since (`restoreReport`), for the next read. A take-in that is refused or has no answer holds the output back with the reason (the run ended, the pause, another agent or member, a read no longer readable, or another refusal); the registry keeps the take-in's refusal texts (`KEPT_REFUSALS`, per method). The folder remembers, for the last 256 reading actions, how many boundaries had released at each one's first take-in and how it answered: a retry is shown only once that take-in answered as a new one and no boundary released more since, else it is held back with no call; the core's replay for an action it does not remember is held back, and so is every retry of it (`read again`); a first take-in with no valid answer is forgotten, so its retry asks again. Nothing is asked while nothing ran in the folder. The shell keeps its one read cursor per command, and `list` still shows a folder command's output only to its own run. The native host never calls the wrapper. Tests in `test/cloud-company-computer-command-folder.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-shell.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-registry-http.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-linux-guest.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-computer-tools.test.ts` and `test/cloud-native-host-dispatch.test.ts`.
- An agent's change to the company's pages and tables shows in its conversation, to be undone there, whatever tool or machine carried it (Interfaces I10, runtime and SQL; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §7, §9 decision 2, §10 row I10; contract 2026-10-02 amendment, item 7; migration `20260930370000`, SQL test 450; the web draws it in a later PR). `qorpera_folder_note_interface_changes(p_binding, p_request: {})` notes each of the binding run's commits (the run's agent as actor, with a `seq`) that changed a path under `interfaces/` or `tables/` and has no note yet, in `seq` order, so a call notes what an earlier one missed. A table's rows (`tables/<name>/rows.csv`, `table_write`'s data) are no part of a note. A note is one `session_info` event (`qorpera.interface-change/v1`: the commit, its `seq`, the run, the agent and each path version the commit made there, with the version before it) whose id comes from the commit (its hash, shaped as a version 8 UUID, which the web's strict event check takes); the call answers `{schema: qorpera.interface-change-notes/v1, noted}`, the number it made, 0 for a run without a conversation (a silent routine's) or one whose conversation closed under the session lock; the run check refuses a run whose conversation closed before. It locks as a run that writes its conversation does: the run's reads (rank 10), then its session (rank 11). The folder client asks it as `noteInterfaceChanges` (a run effect, asked again after a deadlock): `save_interface` after its commit and on every answer that commits nothing (a repeat finds the files saved); `company_write`, `company_mkdir` and `company_delete` after a commit whose path is under `interfaces/` or `tables/` in any case, a replay's too; `apply_proposal` after it applies a proposal that names such a path (or moves something there), a replay's too, and when a later call finds that proposal no longer open, so the repeat of a call whose note failed notes it; and the company runtime once after a run that had a computer, for what its shell and screen saved, when the machine is closed, whether the run succeeded or not. A tool's note that fails after its commit leaves the call unconfirmed (`TOOL_OUTCOME_UNCONFIRMED`, never a refusal), and its repeat notes it; on `save_interface`'s answer that commits nothing, a failure is a refusal; the computer's note that fails is logged by its category and the run's outcome stands. `qorpera_folder_note_interface_changes` joins the path control's list, the gateway's union and the native host's calls (scope `binding`). Runtime tests in `test/cloud-folder-interface-writes.test.ts`, `test/cloud-folder-file-tools.test.ts`, `test/cloud-folder-proposals.test.ts`, `test/cloud-native-company-runtime.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-folder-client.test.ts`, `test/cloud-company-path-control.test.ts` and `test/cloud-native-host-dispatch.test.ts`.
- Each company's names on a node's agent exit (agent exit E8b-2; no SQL, no runtime code; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-EXIT.md` §10, §12; `deploy/agent-exit/README.md` "Each company's names"). A names reconciler (`qorpera-agent-exit-names.service`, root) reads the rules reader's file and the client map every 2 seconds, runs a dnsmasq of its own for each company with rules (`qorpera-agent-exit-resolver@<port>.service` in the exit's namespace, on a port kept in `names/ports.json`, never enabled) that answers its blocked names "no such name", reloads it on a change, and sends each verified machine's lookups to its company's resolver or the exit's own through the nft map `dns_port`. It ends a machine's open DNS connections when its resolver changes, and every machine's once in each exit table it has not seen (the exit's reload makes one again); on start it reloads every resolver that runs. A machine whose company is not known yet or has rules that cannot be used gets no answers. All resolvers write the one `dns.log`; the logs tool keys a query by its process too. `verify` fails on the reconciler's problems; `up` restarts resolvers left in an old namespace and `down` stops them. ROLLOUT.md step 1 enables the names unit; step 9 adds it to a node that runs the exit. Proven in Docker and in an LXD VM with machines of two companies (`test/prove-names-vm.sh`).
- An agent's `table_write` changes a claim for the member who supervises its area (Interfaces I9b; no SQL: the folder core's claim check at the commit is migration `20260930371000`'s; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §5.1, §10 "Built: I9b, an agent's claims"). For a change that touches a needs_yes cell it reads `tables/areas`, takes the `supervisor` of the row whose `agents` names the agent, and lets the agent put that member in an empty cell or empty a cell that holds them; anything else is still refused before the receipt, and an area's `owner`, `supervisor` and `agents` stay the members'. The answer is released with the areas' files too. When the folder core refuses the claim at the commit (the supervisor's consent is not live), the answer gives the claim's next step. The tool's description and the claim's next step say the new rule.
- Agents join a supervised area by its supervisor's yes (Interfaces I9b; migration `20260930372000`, SQL test 454; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §5.1, §6.1 rule 9, §10 "Built: I9b, adding agents by offer"; `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Supervision consent"). While an area's consent is live, a save of `tables/areas/rows.csv` that adds an agent to the row's `agents` is refused (`areas`) unless the consent's member makes it; removing one stays free, and so does an area nobody supervises. Anyone else who may write the rows offers a hired agent to the supervisor with `qorpera_offer_interface_agent`: an offer like a cell's, with `agent` set and `to` the supervisor, one open per row and agent. The supervisor's save of the rows that puts the agent in the row accepts it (a definition saved alone accepts none), the offers' decline and withdraw close it, and it lapses when the consent ends. Every offer's JSON now carries `agent` (null for a cell's), and a cell's settle leaves agents' offers alone.
- An area's agents act for its supervisor while that member consents (Interfaces I9b; migration `20260930371000`, SQL tests 451–453 and 458; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §5.1, §6.1 rule 8, §10 "Built: I9b"; `supabase/CLIENT-CONTRACT.md` "Supervision consent"). A member's own save of `tables/areas/rows.csv` that puts them in a row's `supervisor` cell (a take, or an offer of the cell they accept) is their consent, kept in `qorpera_interface_supervisions`; it ends released, gone (the row deleted or its key changed), withdrawn or left (the member leaves the company), and the owners and admins, the supervisor and the area's owner are told (`interface_supervision_ended`), never the member who ended it. While it is live, the claim rule's *self* for an agent in that row's `agents` is the supervisor, whoever started the run: a commit takes and releases claimed cells for them and nobody else, and an agent's commit in `tables/` holds the areas' index row so no save of the areas changes whom it acts for before it ends. An agent still never changes an area's `owner`, `supervisor` or `agents`. Members withdraw, consent again and list with `qorpera_withdraw_interface_supervision`, `qorpera_consent_interface_supervision` and `qorpera_member_interface_supervisions`. An area's `supervisor` can now be offered. Claims through `table_write` and adding agents by offer are the entries above; the agents' offers, work alone and clearing a leaver's cells come next.
- The machine guest helper shows a boot's company folder at `/company` (F3 step 6c; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-TEST-MACHINES.md` "Mounts in the VM"; nothing hands a view over until 6d): `cloud-machine-guest.mjs <nonce> folder-mount` reads the view `{environmentId, viewId, pass}` from exec stdin (at most 4 KiB, within 15 seconds; never an argument), refuses unless the executor claimed the machine in this boot and `/run` is memory (`MACHINE_FOLDER_NOT_IN_MEMORY`), writes it to the folder program's root-only view file `/run/qorpera-folder/view.json` (0600, its state folder `/run/qorpera-folder/state` empty), starts the image's `qorpera-machine-folder.service` and answers `qorpera.machine-guest-folder/v1` with `mounted`, `closed` (the program exited 3) or `unanswered`. The newest view wins, as the platform's open supersedes the one before (the executor opens again after its restart, or after the view lapsed): the same view again only starts a stopped program; another view stops the program (its mount goes with it), resets the unit if it failed (exit 3), empties the state folder, writes the new view and starts it again (`MACHINE_FOLDER_STILL_MOUNTED` if the old mount stays after the stop). One `folder-mount` runs at a time: from its look at the view held to its answer it holds an flock (util-linux `flock` on the open file it shares with the helper, so the lock goes with the helper's process) on the root-only `/run/qorpera-folder/lock`, waits at most 60 seconds for one still running and is otherwise `MACHINE_FOLDER_BUSY`; a retry or an executor restart overlapping one still running can no longer empty its live state folder. `mounted` needs the unit active and the program's mount on top of `/company`, so a mount whose program ended is not mounted. The unit runs the folder program at `/company` in `qorpera-control.slice`, with no environment, and starts it again after every exit but 3 (`Restart=always`, `RestartPreventExitStatus=3`, no start limit). The pass is in no argument, unit environment, reply or log.
- A new company machine image generation (F3 step 6c; `deploy/machine/build-machine-image.sh`, built by the coordinator at a cut): the toolbox every Qorpera machine has (`RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md`), which the image lacked in part: `tsc` and `tsx` (npm, exact versions), uv and mise (their releases, pinned by sha-256; mise installs any other language or version in the user's home, without root), ripgrep, fd (`fdfind` linked as `fd`), sqlite3, zip and LibreOffice without its desktop parts (`libreoffice-nogui`), beside `fuse3`, the folder program at `/opt/qorpera-folder/qorpera-folder` (the build `deploy/folder-service/folder-program-linux-x64.json` records, pinned by the script as the shared-execution installer pins it), its unit `qorpera-machine-folder.service` (static: only `folder-mount` starts it) and an empty `/company`. `scripts/package-machine-guest.mjs` now needs `--folder-program <built qorpera-folder>` and refuses any other bytes than the recorded build. The image's own checks run every toolbox command with `--version` (unzip with `-v`, it has no `--version`), check the program's pin and version, and verify the folder unit. The scripts tests check the toolbox list, the pin and the 64 KiB helper bound.
- Each node keeps its companies' network rules (agent exit E8b-1; no SQL: E8a's migration `20260930230000` has the table, the read and the role; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-EXIT.md` §10, §12). `@qorpera/runtime/agent-exit-rules` (`agent-exit-rules.ts`, service entry `agent-exit-rules-main.ts`) is a small Node service with `pg`: every 2 s it lists the companies of the exit's client map and calls `qorpera_agent_exit_network_rules` as the role `qorpera_agent_exit` (at most 10,000 companies a call, and once even with none), over TLS with Supabase's CA (`sslmode=verify-full`). It keeps `rules.json` (`qorpera.agent-exit-rules/v1`: per company `null` for no rules file, or its version, mode, block and allow) and keeps the last rules while a pass cannot read, also across a restart; `status.json` says when it last read and names a problem as a code. `scripts/package-agent-exit-rules.mjs` bundles it with esbuild for the nodes, and the exit's installer takes the package. Its unit is `qorpera-agent-exit-rules.service` (a dynamic user with no capabilities, on the node's own network, with the login and the CA as credentials). `deploy/agent-exit/rules-reader-login.py` gives the role a new login and places it on every node without anyone handling it. Nothing reads `rules.json` yet; E8b-2 will.
- The Company Computer types a signed hook's secret into the sender's settings (folder core C4, E3b-3; no SQL: E3b-1's migration `20260930160000` has the read; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5.7; agreed with the coordinator on 6 October 2026, with the coordinator's check of the box's type). `computer_browser` takes `{action: vault_fill, role: textbox, name, hook, field: signing_secret}` beside the account's form, and `manage_hook`'s description says how to use it on the hook's `secretSite`. The native host sends such a call to the company's secret broker on its own route (`POST /v1/event-hook-fills`, `qorpera.event-hook-fill-request/v1`, with the run's lease in `x-qorpera-identity`, which the route proves first as on `/v1/event-hooks`; `createHttpCompanyComputerSessionFactory` takes `hookFills`, and without it such a call is refused before the office). The broker reads the secret through `qorpera_fill_event_hook_secret`, checks the database's context field by field (`qorpera.event-hook-fill-context/v1`), opens the envelope and seals `{value, hosts: [site]}` to the computer's fill key with A1-3's box, whose identity now names either an account or a hook (`CompanyAccountFillIdentity`), so the two never open as each other; the answer is `qorpera.event-hook-fill-answer/v1` or E3b-2's refusal. `EventHookAuthority` requires `readEventHookFill`, and the broker opens its hook routes only with all three hook functions. The office opens the box with the call's own hook, types only on the site over https, and makes the box (an `input` of type `text` or `password`) a password box before the value arrives: it reads the type back, and again just before typing, and refuses unless it is `password` (`ACCOUNT_FILL_NOT_HIDDEN`, nothing typed); a box that shows its text again after the typing is emptied and refused the same way. The secret is then hidden from every snapshot, title and address, as a password is. The review card reads "Type a hook's signing secret into a web page" and names the hook and the field.
- Run logs in the company folder (agent memory tree W1b-1; migration `20260930231000`, SQL tests 951 and 952; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §7, §8). A run's log is `runs/<run>/` under its conversation's tree root: `run.toml`, `log/<8 digits>.jsonl` (16 steps a block) and the run's own tree `t<n>/<8 digits>.jsonl`, indexed in `qorpera_memory_run_logs` and `qorpera_memory_run_steps` (with the folder keys each step names, for erasure). A memory commit's request names its log (`runLog`) and its run's generation (`runGenerations`); a run writes only its own log, steps only grow, and a narrow log takes only narrow lines. The native host gets three calls: `qorpera_memory_run_log_place` (where the log is or goes), `qorpera_memory_step_hashes` (a narrow step's result hashes, under the run's lease) and `qorpera_folder_memory_commit` as the run. The runtime's writer is W1c-3.
- A run resumes after a cut-off, and pauses at its usage limit, from its log (agent memory tree W5a-2; migration `20260930270000`, SQL tests 990 and 994; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10.1, §12, §14 W5). W5a-1's gate (`qorpera_run_resumes_from_log`, off until now) is on: a run takes the resume path only when it has a live log, no step erase is open on it and it is not narrow; otherwise a cut-off run still ends in review and a run at its usage limit still fails. While a step erase is open on a run's log, no attempt of the run starts: the runner's accept and start refuse with 55006 `memory_erase_open` (runner-control answers 503, the runner's start fails and the assignment runs out), and the orchestrator's claim skips the job until the erase closes. Nothing changes live until runs write logs (W1c-3).
- Agents open GitHub and HMAC hooks on folder-v2, with a secret the vault makes (folder core C4, E3b-1 and E3b-2; migration `20260930160000`, SQL tests 987 and 948; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5.7; agreed with the coordinator on 6 October 2026). `manage_hook`'s `create` takes `proof` `github` or `hmac-sha256` and `site`, the host the secret may be typed into (`github.com` for GitHub unless named, required for HMAC); Stripe is still refused. The folder client sends such a create to the company's secret broker (`POST /v1/event-hooks`, `qorpera.event-hook-request/v1`, with the run's lease in `x-qorpera-identity`, which the route proves as G2-6a's other broker routes do before the database is asked anything), which draws the secret (GitHub: 32 random bytes as hex; HMAC: `whsec_` and their base64), seals it under the company's key and opens the hook with it in one database step (`qorpera_folder_open_signed_hook`). The answer is the hook's receipt with `secretSite`, or the database's refusal in its own words (`qorpera.event-hook-refusal/v1`); the secret never leaves the vault and the model never sees it. A retry with the same operation key answers from the receipt, and its own secret is never stored. A host without the broker refuses a signed hook as unavailable. The review card names who signs and the site. The fill that types the secret into the site is E3b-3.
- A worker's parent step is the dispatching call's own step in the run's log (agent memory tree W4a-2; migration `20260930271000`, SQL test 995; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §12, §14 W4). `manage_worker`'s dispatch and follow-up requests to `qorpera_folder_worker_commit` that name `parentStep` also name `parentCall`, the tool call id the step line records as `call` (1 to 256 characters, no control characters), and each comes only with the other; otherwise "Worker request is invalid" (22023).
