The step must be a `manage_worker` step with that call id in the sending run's live log, neither finished nor erased; otherwise "The parent step is not this call's pending step in the run's log" (22023), and nothing changes. A request that names neither is checked as before. Nothing changes live until runs write logs (W1c-3) and `manage_worker` sends its step and call id (W4b).
- Agents open hooks and mailboxes on folder-v2 (folder core C4, E3a; migration `20260930106000`, SQL tests 939 and 985; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §5; agreed with the coordinator on 6 October 2026). A folder-v2 run's agent gets `manage_hook`: `create` with kind `hook` (an address hook: name, purpose, and optionally `maxBodyBytes` and `perMinute`, SQL's defaults otherwise) or kind `mailbox` (`localPart`, purpose; the hook is named `mail-<localPart>`), or `pause`, `resume` or `remove` of a hook by its name. The tool checks E1's rules word for word before anything is sent and says which one a request broke (`INVALID_ARGUMENTS: …`); a signed proof (Stripe, GitHub, HMAC) is refused there as coming later (E3b, E3c). It calls `qorpera_folder_manage_hook(p_binding, p_request)` through the folder client (`manageHook`), a run effect under its own receipt keyed by the operation key, so a retry replays it; a creation's answer, and its replay, read the address, path and URL from the hook row while the hook is live, and the receipt never stores the address. Only a run whose requester is an owner or admin may change hooks (as E1's Settings calls and as `list_hooks` shows addresses); a worker never does. SQL's refusals (the caps of 50 hooks and 20 mailboxes, a taken name or address, no company mail, no such hook, a removed hook, the role, the wake's refusals, and a run removing the hook whose event woke it, which E2a's gate would then refuse: it may pause it instead) reach the model in its words as `REFUSED: …`; after an answer that was lost or did not check, a refusal for the same key is an unknown outcome. The action review calls a creation `external` (the outside can then reach the company), a pause or resume `internal`, and a removal `destructive` (it is final, and a removed mailbox's address is free for any company to take), and its card names the hook or mailbox, its purpose and what the change does. `qorpera_folder_manage_hook` joins the path control's list, the gateway's union, the native host's calls (scope `binding`) and the observation vocabulary (`manage_hook`).
- An offline shell command gets a company folder view of its own (F3 step 4, slice 4b, SQL; C1b-3b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md` "Slices", `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1B.md` §3, §8; migration `20260930183000`, SQL test 981; the folder service and the office runtime use it from later slices). `qorpera_runtime_office_shell_commands` is keyed by the start effect's receipt (`id = receipt_id`) and keeps the guest's session id in the new `command_id`; the native operation's begin of a shell start makes the row before the guest runs anything, with the screen's generation, the run's member and `network`. A shell start may carry a boolean `network` (absent: the command is offline). The office's 30 s report fills in `command_id` and leaves a row it has not named yet for 60 seconds after the start's effect ended; only commands it named are told to the agent as stopped. The view's kind `computer_shell_command` opens for an offline command that has not ended while its run holds the screen, one per command; it acts through the run while the run's job is leased, then as the agent, company-wide like a desktop, and stands while the screen keeps the command's generation, the office the view's activation and the agent its access. Once the command has ended it saves only a final change set: the view actor's `"final": true`, which closes the view in the commit's transaction, saved or not; only a command view sends one.
- `manage_routine` on folder-v2 takes an `event` trigger (folder core C4, E2b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §4.4): `hookId` (a hook or mailbox from `list_hooks`), optional `only` (the event types to take), `timeZone` and `report`. It writes `when: event`, `hook`, `only` and the time zone into the routine's file, and the save's own rules refuse a hook that is not a live one of the company and a ChatGPT plan as payer. Its folder-v2 description now offers it, and says that a file_change or event routine listens from its last save, so a later save drops what was not delivered yet. Before, folder-v2 agents were told that event routines were not available. The description's folder-v2 wording of keys, lists, removal and conflicts is shorter, so it stays within the tool boundary's 4096 bytes.
- Agents read interfaces and tables (Interfaces I8a; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §10 "Built: I8a"; `cloud-folder-interface-tools.ts`; migration `20260928529100`, no SQL test): a folder-v2 run gets two Company File tools. `interface_read` (`interface`, `view`, `after`) gives one view of `interfaces/<name>/interface.toml` a page at a time: the settings and buttons first, then each block as the page draws it (a board's part and a calendar's source are one item each), with a table's rows narrowed and ordered as the page keeps them, lookup columns filled, a calendar's days in the run's time zone and a file's text; mail, the folder's own todos, routines and human tasks, and outside content get a note naming where to read them, and live state a note that it is not shown; a broken interface lists its problems, and one whose access lists other agents shows nothing. `table_read` (`table`, `where`, `after`, `limit`) gives `tables/<name>/` rows a page at a time by key; its first page holds each column's type, the table's problems and who may write it. Both parse the text as it is now with the run's own reads, cut what doesn't fit one result and say how much more there is, and release exactly the answer with the files it shows (a folder at its version). The run's observations name the four I8 tools; `table_write` and `save_interface` come with I8b.
- Agents save interfaces and write table rows (Interfaces I8b; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-INTERFACES.md` §10 "Built: I8b"; `cloud-folder-interface-writes.ts`; no migration, no SQL test): a folder-v2 run gets two more Company File tools, and a worker `table_write` only (none when its access is read). `save_interface` (`operationKey`, `files`: each a path under `interfaces/` or `tables/`, its text and `expectedVersion`) saves interfaces' and tables' settings, READMEs and `interfaces/order.md` as one change set through the folder core, under the call's receipt. `table_write` (`table`, `operationKey`, `set`, `remove`) sets rows by key, adds rows (an id column left out gets an id made from the run, the operation key and the row's place, given in the answer's `ids`) and removes rows by exactly their key, editing `rows.csv` in place: every byte it doesn't change, line ends and a byte order mark included, stays. A row write refuses a claim the agent has no supervisor to act for, a table whose `[write]` doesn't name the agent, a table whose rows don't all fit and rows over 64 KiB, each as an answer before its receipt. The tool boundary gains `prepare` (`CloudNativeTool`): a tool may read what the call changes before the automatic review, and its value replaces the parsed arguments for the review, the work log and the effect class. `save_interface` uses it to read the files it replaces and give the review a card of what changes (never the files) and its effect: a new interface or table, a button, an access, columns or `[write]` is a delegation and runs only with the review's approval (the review maps an unprepared call to a delegation, and `table_write` is internal); anything else applies at once. Units go in the order the folder core needs: an interface that stops being main before the one that becomes main, and a removed column out of `rows.csv` (once optional and empty in every row) before the definition without it. A file that already holds the call's text is named as unchanged. A number is written as decimal text, never in exponent form (`5e-7`), which the reader refuses. Every answer that says what a file holds is released with the entries it read, the commit's answer too; one that cannot be released after the commit is unconfirmed (`TOOL_OUTCOME_UNCONFIRMED`), never a refusal, so the model repeats the same call and its replay answers. A replay of a saved call finds its change made and answers that nothing changed (a made id is made again, so it finds its own row); a call that waited for a member or failed at its commit replays from its receipt. `company_write`'s refusal of a reviewed file now names `save_interface`. `cloud-folder-file-tools.ts` exports `OPERATION_KEY`, `TOOL_VERSION`, `releaseId`, `path`, `operationKey` and `version`, and `cloud-folder-interface-tools.ts` exports `readFile`, `readTable` (now with `rowsText`), `problemText` and `clip`. Runtime tests in `test/cloud-folder-interface-writes.test.ts`, `test/cloud-action-review.test.ts` and `test/cloud-worker-tools.test.ts`.
- A run's step lines (agent context tree W2c; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.3, §4.5, §5.7, §7.2, §7.5; `memory-tree/step-lines.ts`, no SQL): `CloudRunStepLines` builds the summary tree of the run's own log in the run's lane and stores it beside the log in `runs/<run>/t<n>/`. Each batch of steps the log commits (`onCommitted`) starts a pass, one at a time: it builds every line `[0, T)` is missing (`memoryCompact`, kind `run`, each level-0 window the run's raw steps `[w(j), j)`) and commits them through the log's `commitTree` at the run's generation as the writer read it before its first step or line (§4.3; a read that found the log anew is read again), in change sets of at most 64 blocks and 1 MiB, reading each block again first and keeping its stored lines. A resumed run's earlier steps (`earlier`) are built like its own, and their stored lines are used, not built again. A step is rendered by `memoryRenderStep`; an erased step is its tool and `(erased)`, an earlier process's narrow step its tool's name only, and an earlier process's step with no result shows `outcome unknown: check before you repeat it` (`cloudRunStepText`); a step of this process still waiting for its result is built once it has one. This process's narrow steps are in full in its own view, and no line over them, or over their window, is stored (§4.5). A node the compactor failed on with its inputs at hand stays missing for the rest of the run, and the merges over it are not tried (§7.2); a stopped lane, a deadline or an unreadable step is no failure. A commit refused for the run's generation (or while an erase is open), or a generation that moved, discards the batch whole and nothing more is stored in the run: what was built serves this run's own view, and the next run builds it again. A `stale` commit whose block is no longer at the version it was read at (another writer's, or a log that moved under the pass) is read and committed again, up to 3 times, and what is left waits for the next pass; `gone` ends the writer. `lines(r, budget, signal)` waits up to SETTLE for missing level-0 lines and returns the view (`memoryView`) as `start+size|text` rows, a placeholder for a line not built; `composeContext()` is W2b's composer over it. `finish(signal)`, after the log's `close`, builds what is left within CLOSE and commits it under its own signal; after it, nothing more is built. `observe` gets each pass's `MemoryCompactStats` for `memory_compaction`. Nothing constructs it yet: W1c-3 wires it into `runCloudCodexToolSession`, and M1b part 4 the compactor call. Runtime tests in `test/memory-tree/step-lines.test.ts`.
- Summary trees, M1b part 4a: the primary view at run start (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §5.3, §5.5, §6.1, §6.2, §13; `memory-tree/compose.ts`, migration `20260930204000`, SQL test 946; no company is listed yet: the coordinator lists each once M1b part 4b writes its trees). An agent's run in its own thread on folder-v2 (a room wake whose final send is required, company audience, not a worker) asks `qorpera_memory_view`, a new native host call with scope binding (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`, standard), alongside its notebook. It answers null unless the run has a conversation and its company is in `qorpera_memory_tree_companies`; otherwise the conversation, T (its included events before the first event of the run's inputs and its wake's `inputIds`, or, with none, those up to the transcript start), the sequence of T's last event, and the conversation's live tree. The run folds `[0, T)` from the tree's lines into the developer message after the head, lists the branches (`qorpera_memory_branches`), and puts `conversationMessages` before `currentInputs` and `branches` after them in the context message, whose `history` then keeps only the messages after T's last one (`historyTruncated` holds only when none was left out). `cloudNativeStableInstructions` is the developer instructions up to the head and the view, which `compileCloudNativeCompanyInstructions` follows with the wake's. Once the context is hashed, the blocks shown are released (`memoryRelease`, call `composition:0`, under the context's SHA-256), and the first request is recorded in `qorpera_memory_views` through `qorpera_memory_view_record` (scope binding: step, messages, head, view and branches bytes, placeholders, and the stable prefix's SHA-256; a step is recorded once per attempt, keyed by the lease's assignment, so a pristine retry or a resume records afresh), which answers the folder clock: with the conversations shown, the view reference W4 will pass to workers (`MemoryViewReference`, at most 256 KiB of its jsonb text, as `memoryJsonbTextBytes` measures it). A failed view request is logged and the run keeps its history; a failed release fails the run; a failed record is logged and leaves the run without a view reference. Only a run with the view has `zoom` in its tool list, and every model request it makes carries the conversation's prompt cache key (`memoryCacheKeyComposer`; `runCloudNativeRunToolSession` takes `composeContext`). The path control and the gateway permit both calls (16 MiB answers for the view and the branches). `memory-tree/branches.ts` exports `memoryTreeRead`, `MemoryTreeRead` and `memoryBlockKey`, and `memory-tree/zoom.ts` exports `memoryRelease`.
- Summary trees, M1b part 3a: the zoom's SQL (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §8, §10; migration `20260930202000`, SQL tests 943 and 944; the `zoom` tool itself comes in part 3b). `qorpera_memory_zoom`, a native host call with scope binding, resolves a ref (`.`, `thread:<agent>`, `group:<group>`; `run:<id>` names none until W1 adds run logs), checks the line, stores the receipt by target and range, holds the target session `FOR SHARE` and reads: the conversation's live tree with its blocks, or the events by message number with their speaker, text, quotes, attachments and the run that wrote them. An erased event keeps only its speaker. `zoom` joins `CLOUD_RUNTIME_TOOL_OPERATIONS` (and the observation vocabulary), `cloudActionEffect` classes it as a read, and `qorpera_memory_zoom` joins `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` as standard.
- Summary trees, M4 parts a and b: a run's branches (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §5.5; migration `20260930220000`, SQL test 970; `memory-tree/branches.ts`, not wired in yet: M4c does it after M1b part 4). `qorpera_memory_branch_list` chooses an agent's branches in its environment, the most recently active first: its own thread, the group chats (`agents/groups/<group>/group.md`) it is an active member of, and for the Co-Founder every other agent's thread, each live as zoom finds it and never the run's own conversation. `qorpera_memory_branches`, a native host call with scope binding that joins `NATIVE_HOST_CALLS` as standard, answers with the run's branches and the live trees (`qorpera_memory_tree_read`) of the sessions the request names, in one statement; it stores no receipt. `memoryRunBranches` asks for the list, then for the trees `BRANCH_READS` (8) at a time while BRANCHES has room for another branch, and folds them as `memoryBranches` does with every tree read (`memoryBranches` now takes the first source's index and the room left). Each branch is named by `memoryConversationName` ("Bo (thread)", "Launch (group chat)"). A tree read later brings its own message count, and a branch that is gone by then shows no line built. It returns each branch read as a worker's view reference names a conversation (`{sessionId, generation, through, blocks}`, §12): the erase generation its tree was read at, in the same statement as its lines, its count and the blocks its lines show; and all those blocks, each once, for the composer to release with the primary view's. Runtime tests in `test/memory-tree/branches.test.ts`.
- Summary trees, M1b part 3b: the `zoom` tool (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §8, §9, §10; `memory-tree/zoom.ts`, migration `20260930203000`, SQL test 945; not in the tool list yet: M1b part 4 puts it there). `memoryZoomTool` opens a conversation folded to one page (`.`, `thread:<agent>`, `group:<group>` or a branch's ref), a line into its two halves with their dates, or a line of size 1 into the original event with its author, time, quotes, attachments, card and run. Its text is fitted to the fence after encoding and, when text is left, ends with `nextOffset` and the total, which the agent passes back as `from`. Its description and schema are the same for every run. The summary lines a result shows are released before it is returned through `qorpera_memory_release`, a new native host call with scope binding (`NATIVE_HOST_CALLS`, standard): the run's read record of those blocks by path and version, under the nil resource with platform authority, which the run's re-check and a coworker wake's parent-reads check leave out and a wake's read copy copies as it is. A release id is derived from the run, the call, the text and the blocks, so a retry replays it; `recordCloudRunLogRelease` records it for the call's step, whose reads are now only paths and versions. `read_context`'s schema no longer lists the run's ids and hashes (they stay in its prompt, and its arguments are checked against them), so it too is the same for every run.
- Summary trees, M1b part 4b-1: the compactor's lane (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §7.1, §7.2; no SQL; not called yet: closing comes in part 4b-3). `runCloudCodexToolSession` takes `compactor`, a `MemoryCompactChannel` (`memory-tree/compact.ts`), and attaches to it a `MemoryCompactCall` for its run: each call opens a fresh, ephemeral thread in the run's own Codex app-server (`COMPACT_MODEL`, the run's workspace, read-only, no tools, the call's system prompt as its developer instructions), and each `send` is one turn of it at `COMPACT_EFFORT`, answered with the model's text and the tokens it used (input, cached and output, by how far the thread's cumulative usage grew since its previous reply; none when Codex reported none). A failed send carries no usage: the thread's `unreported()` (new and optional on `MemoryCompactThread`) gives what its failed sends used, and `memoryCompactLine` counts it into the batch's stats. The compactor's model requests pass the result fence on a lane of their own, as a reviewer's do: they are never composed from the agent's run, never recorded as its context and never counted as its requests, and an output one carries is answered `{"available":false}` (which the pinned Codex takes for no tool result: it fails the turn); a request its thread makes is refused. Side threads (reviewers and compactor threads) now start one at a time, so a prewarm before a thread's id is that thread's, while a known side thread's late prewarm stays on its own lane. The result fence takes up to 24 connections at once (was 8; exported as `CLOUD_NATIVE_FENCE_MAX_CONNECTIONS`): a prewarm and a turn's request, each with its composition, for the agent, a reviewer and `JOBS` compactor threads. A provider usage limit (or a ChatGPT plan's 429) rejects a send with `MemoryCompactStop` `usage-limit`, another 429 with `rate-limit`, and a 5xx with `outage`; any other failed turn, a cancelled send (its turn is interrupted) or a reply over 16 KiB fails only that send, and a failed send ends its thread. When the run fails or its process closes, open sends stop with the run's provider signal (an outage when there is none), and the channel is detached, so a later call stops as an outage. A new `closing` option runs after the agent's verified turn and before the process ends; the compactor's lane stays open for it, and its failure leaves the run's result as it was.
- Summary trees, M1b part 2: the compactor's core (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §3, §7.1–§7.5; `@qorpera/runtime/memory-tree/compact` and `/render`, not wired in yet). `memoryCompact` builds one batch of missing lines: a source's leaves and every merge above them that `[0, T)` completes. A free node needs no call; a level-0 line sees its window (the raw events `[w(i), i)`, each its own element cut to COMPACT_EVENT with `[…]`, never a line) and its event whole; a merge sees its two children once both exist. Events and lines between tags are escaped as the view's are (`escapeCloudNativeContextJson`), so none can close its tag or open another. The shared rules name those escapes (`\u003c` for `<` and so on) so a line carries the characters themselves. Each line is one fresh thread whose system prompt is `COMPACT` (`COMPACT_STEP` for a run's log) and a 512-byte line for scale, on auto-review's model at low effort. A reply over NODE goes back with its size and the line cut at the limit, up to TRIES, and the shortest is kept within LINE_BYTES as stored; an empty reply (which is how a refusal comes back), an error or a line still too long leaves the node missing. A thread is closed once, even when it opens only after an abort; a close that throws is dropped. `MemoryCompactLane` runs a run's calls JOBS at a time, level 0 in event order; a `MemoryCompactStop` (usage limit, rate limit or outage) stops every call in it, and the batch's deadline stops what still runs; what was built is kept. Free nodes are still built after a stop. After the deadline they are built only from what was read before it: a free leaf whose event was not read in time stays missing. `MemoryCompactStats` counts calls, free lines, tokens, overshoots and failures for `memory_compaction`. `memoryRenderEvent` renders a conversation's event (`<speaker>: <text>`, the attachments, then each quote's JSON-escaped record) and `memoryRenderStep` a run's step. The model call is passed in; M1b part 4 wires it to the run's Codex app-server. Runtime tests in `test/memory-tree/compact.test.ts`.
- `memory-tree/run-work.ts` composes an agent run's provider requests (agent context tree W2b; design `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §5.7, §6.3, §7.5): `cloudRunWorkComposer` keeps the newest steps raw and folds the earlier ones into one `qorpera.run-work/v1` work message (`cloudRunWorkMessage`, escaped as the prompt is). It folds only at a boundary, when the raw tail passes RUN_VIEW / 2 (32,000 bytes), moving to the first response that brings it under RUN_VIEW / 4 and never past the newest response; a fold ends where a response starts, so a call is never folded without its output nor one response's calls apart, and the work message stays as it is until the next boundary. When a fold moves, the caller's log first commits its finished steps (`steps(signal)`: W1's `flush`, then `steps()`), so the fold names every step it covers; a call the log never holds (refused before it became a step, or erased) is folded with no line. A fold stops at the first item in the work that is not the run's own (a host-written message). An image or audio item counts as `RUN_WORK_MEDIA_BYTES` (4,400 bytes) in the raw tail, and a lone surrogate in a line becomes U+FFFD, which Codex's strict parse needs. Reasoning goes back only to the model that made it, in the process that made it. A resumed run's earlier steps go in the work message with nothing folded. The lines come from the caller (M1b's `fold` over the run's step lines); nothing calls the composer yet (W2c wires it).
- Summary trees, M1b part 1: the pure core (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.1, §4.2, §5.1–§5.5, §6.1, §6.2; `@qorpera/runtime/memory-tree/*`, not wired in yet). `tree` holds the constants (the §5.1 table, with SETTLE and CLOSE in ms, and BRANCH_MIN 600, OVERFLOW 1.25), a line's text as stored (control characters as spaces, lone surrogates as U+FFFD) and its byte size, `node(l, k)`'s address and block path, the free leaf and parent rules, and a line's and a block's JSON in the SQL's key order with M1a's refusals; a run log's lines (`kind` `run`, W1) name no event at any level. `fold` is §5.2's furthest-first fold in `O(T · levels)` (one queue per level), checked against the fold as §5.2 states it; `memoryOverflow` merges the oldest pairs into placeholder parents past VIEW × 1.25, and `memoryView` is both. `view` writes the primary view after `VIEW_DOC` (escaped with the prompt's `escapeCloudNativeContextJson`, no dates), folds each branch within its share (half of BRANCHES for the most recent, then a quarter, each at least BRANCH_MIN, the oldest named by an `earlier|zoom` row, the rest listed), and `memoryPromptCacheKey` keys the prompt cache by the payer's login, the agent and the conversation (§6.2): `qorpera:` and a base64url SHA-256, 51 characters. Budgets count a row as it is sent (`memoryRowBytes`: a JSON string, escaped, and a separator), so markup cannot push a view past its budget. HEAD stays `CLOUD_AGENT_HEAD_BYTES`. Runtime tests in `test/memory-tree`.
- A run log's earlier steps and the run's tree beside it (agent memory tree W1c-2; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5, §7.5; no SQL). `CloudRunLogFolder.commit` takes the memory commit's preconditions as its third parameter, `CloudRunLogPreconditions` (`runLog`, and `runGenerations` for a batch of the run's tree only): a step batch names no generation, so no generation check discards step records. `earlier(signal)` reads a resumed run's steps before its first step, as their lines store them, once, in step order, with an erased step kept in place (`erased: true`, blanks); with no log it throws `CloudRunLogUnavailable` `no_log`, with fewer steps `log_short`, and a gap or an empty block is not a run log. `treeRead(path, signal)` and `commitTree(operationKey, blocks, generation, signal)` read and commit blocks of the run's tree (`CloudRunTreeBlock`: a `t0`–`t14` block path, its text and the version it was read at) under `runs/<run>/t<n>/`, making the tier folders they need, also after `close`. `commitTree` answers `"landed"`, `"stale"` (the generation moved, a step erase is open, or a block is not at the version it was read at) or `"gone"`, finds a moved log again under `<operationKey>:2` to `:4`, and throws `CloudRunLogUnavailable` on any other refusal. Each block lands or not on its own, so a batch that did not land may have landed some of its blocks: a writer reads every block again before it builds the batch again. `generation()` is the run's generation as the log was last found, also by `earlier` and the tree calls, and `CloudRunLogCommitted.generation` carries it to listeners. `steps()` now keeps erased steps (`CloudRunLogStep.erased`) and returns `args` and `text` as stored, cut to their bounds.
- A run keeps its work log in the company folder (agent memory tree W1c-1; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.5): `CloudRunLog` writes `runs/<run>/run.toml` and `log/<8-digit first step>.jsonl` under the session's tree root, one JSON line for every tool call the model sees, refusals before the call runs included (`j`, `tool`, `call`, `args`, `args_bytes`, `args_hash` = the boundary's digest, null for arguments that are not valid JSON or too big, `result` = the envelope the model got, after the release check, `reads`, `reads_cut` when the reads did not fit 16 KiB, `releases`, `text` = the agent's own words since the step before, never reasoning, `at` to the second). Blocks hold at most 16 steps and 64 KiB; batches commit every 16 finished steps or 30 seconds, and at close, through the folder memory commit with the `runLog` precondition, each change set at most 1 MiB and 64 files. An acting call's step commits pending (`"result":null`) before the call runs, once no call before it is still waiting for its answer; when that cannot land the call is denied (`transport_unavailable`), and once the log's folder is gone no call runs (`authority_unavailable`). A denied call that closes what it stood for (P40b, `apply_proposal`) commits its step pending the same way before it closes it; when that cannot land, the denial stands and nothing is closed. Message sends keep `args` empty; their `args_hash` is the plain digest for now, which W1c-3 replaces with the platform's keyed hash before any log is wired, and narrow steps' line shape comes with W1c-3. A conflict reads the block again, a rename relocates the log, and an erased line is never written back. Only the last block and blocks with a pending step stay in memory, and a final step keeps its reads as text only. `onCommitted` tells listeners which steps each change set made final and the blocks it saved. Nothing builds a run with a log yet: the folder port and the run wiring follow in W1c-3.
- Summary trees, M1a: the SQL (`deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §4.2–§4.5, §10; migrations `20260930110000` and `20260930111000`, SQL tests 940–942). A conversation's summary tree is a folder of files at `agents/<agent>/memory/thread` or `agents/groups/<group>/memory`: `tree.toml` (`format`, `conversation`, `node_bytes`) and blocks `t<l>/<8 digits>.jsonl` of up to 30 lines `{l, k, seq?, hash?, free, tries?, text}`, each line at most 2,048 bytes and every level-0 line bound to its event (seq, hash and message number). Only `qorpera_folder_memory_commit` (service_role) writes them: it carries `preconditions.sessionGenerations` into `qorpera_folder_commit`, which takes each named conversation FOR SHARE at the session step and refuses the whole batch with `memory_generation_stale` when an erase raised `qorpera_shared_sessions.memory_generation` meanwhile. Members and agents read and search tree files as any file; anything else that writes, moves or deletes them is refused `platform_only`, and a tree moves or goes only with its agent's or group chat's folder. Each written file is indexed in `qorpera_memory_trees` and `qorpera_memory_tree_nodes`, and erasing a deleted agent's or group chat's folder deletes its retired tree rows; `qorpera_memory_read_receipts` is created for M1b. P29 subscriptions never match a tree path. No runtime code reads them yet (M1b).
- A view's renewal says what the view is and which machine it belongs to (F3 step 4, slice 4a; migration `20260930104000`, SQL test 980; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-COMPANY-MACHINES.md`). `qorpera_folder_view_renew`'s open answer gains `kind` (the view's kind) and `machineId` (the view's machine allocation: the office's activation for a desktop, the coding machine's for a coding session, null for a run box). The node's folder service learns of a view only from a machine that connects with it, so this is how it can serve a desktop to its own office machine alone, and let it write there. The closed answer is unchanged, and the service's parser already ignores fields it does not know.
- Coding sessions on folder-v2 (C3b, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (C3)"): `manage_worker` dispatches and follows up a coding session through the folder worker files, its `brief.md` naming the harness and repository and the request carrying them for the database's checks (C3a); the dispatch answers `coding: {phase: "queued", harness, branch}`. `archive` closes a coding session (`qorpera_folder_archive_coding_worker`, a binding-scope native host call), its refusals (not a coding session, a new turn since the agent was asked) in their words. The folder-v2 tool description offers coding sessions and archive.
- A folder-v2 coding turn's result is written to its `results.md` (C3d, migration `20260930108000`, SQL test 982 and test 928 updated; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (C3)"). `qorpera_complete_coding_turn` and the lease-expired failure in `qorpera_claim_coding_session` mark the worker due (`qorpera_coding_results_files`) in the report's own transaction, and the object gateway's writer claims it, writes the file as the platform and finishes it (`qorpera_claim_coding_results_file`, `qorpera_finish_coding_results_file`, service role only). `qorpera_company_state_worker_rules` lets a platform commit under the due row's exact operation key write a live coding worker's results only as its turn reported them (outcome, report time, no decision, note or files), and `qorpera_company_state_index_worker` points the worker at them and removes the due row. A decide waits while the row is due. The agent's revival waits for the file (`qorpera_coding_results_held` in `qorpera_folder_worker_due` and `qorpera_folder_workers_company`), at most a minute after the report and not after a writing problem; a batch sibling waits with it.
- A folder-v2 coding session's cancel by the run that was asked about it, once the session has a new turn, is refused as archive's is (C3c, migration `20260930107500`): `manage_worker` cancel answers SQL's 55000 in its words (`CODING_QUESTION_STALE`), which `cancelWorker` keeps for the caller.
- Per-request model usage (summary trees M0 part B, for the design's per-request cache checks; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-STEPS-0-2-OBSERVABILITY.md` "Measurement limits"): a Codex turn now records one `model_request` observation, a child of its `model_turn`, for each `thread/tokenUsage/updated` report whose cumulative `total` grew by exactly its `last` from the turn's previous report (zero on the new thread), with `last`'s counters (input, cached input, output, reasoning, total). That is how Codex 0.155.1 adds a completed provider response (`TokenUsageInfo::append_last_usage`); repeats on rate-limit changes, compaction estimates (no input) and a filled context window do not add up that way and record nothing. The `model_turn` keeps the latest total as before. These are Codex's own usage reports, not a billing-exact count. No new calls, no migration (`model_request` is already in the vocabulary, and the run readers already skip it or see it beside rows that were already there), and no change to what runs do.
- Block-history requests erase one memory block's earlier versions (agent memory tree W1a; migration `20260930120000`, SQL test 950; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-AGENT-MEMORY-TREE.md` §10 step 3, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-ERASURE.md` §14): `qorpera_folder_erase_block_history(environment, user, key, block)` (service role) asks P18 as the platform for the member who asked, with the reserved key `memtree:<sha256 of table:id:folded key>`. While another erasure's versions phase covers a path the request names, every request (with a cutoff, a life or neither) answers 55006 `Block history is being erased; ask again` (retry), under the path lock and before the count: that phase takes versions in no order, so nothing left could otherwise be answered before the block's history is gone. The block is cut at the delete it names; once that delete was erased by a later erasure, past its versions phase, the request answers nothing left. With no cutoff every life of a block not live goes; a block live again, or renamed to a live path since, is refused with 55000 `Block is live again`, whose detail `{live, life}` names the live path and the key's newest life. That life is the tree built again by the next run or another tree's block at a reused name (a member's restore leaves tree paths out), so the carrier asks again with that life: the block is cut at the life's start, every earlier life goes however it ended (deleted, or renamed to a path deleted since), and the life stays (refused the same way under the path lock if the key has had a newer life since, and the carrier goes round again). Its partners through moves are added by the wrapper, in a request with a cutoff or a life or for an older life a life that moved on to a live path is left alone, every copy is named, and nothing left answers `erasureId` null with no row. The row keeps `block_history`, and after done its digest, copies and `for`; phases 1 to 3 note no run for it. `qorpera_folder_erasure_past_versions(id)` says when a request is past P18's versions phase. A member's erasure that names a tree's `tree.toml` alone (`agents/<agent>/memory/thread/tree.toml`, `agents/groups/<group>/memory/tree.toml`) is refused with 22023 `A tree's tree.toml is erased with its tree`: a message erase finds a conversation's earlier tree roots by that file's bytes.
- SECURITY: an upload's soft quota no longer tells an actor whether the company stores a hash (folder core; migration `20260930062000`, SQL test 914; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §3). Near the company's storage limit, `qorpera_folder_authorize_object_upload` let a chunk through when the catalog had it and refused any other (413), so the hash alone told whether the company stores bytes the actor may not read. Now the quota is waived only for an object the actor possesses under its intent (`held` at open, or `put` by it), and any other object is checked as new, stored or not. The 409 for an object being collected stays (backlog); 201 vs 200 on a put is accepted as the dedup channel.
- Folder core P40a (C2-R2, migration `20260930074000`, SQL tests 926 and 927; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C2.md` §8): a view's unit that needs review and carries none is parked as a proposal (`waiting_for_review`, reason `parked`) instead of denied. The agent's next run for the same member lists it (`qorpera_folder_agent_proposals`), reviews it as one `apply_proposal` call through the gate, records the approval through R1's recorder, and applies it (`qorpera_folder_apply_proposal`). A deny declines it (`qorpera_folder_close_proposal`); the agent's run, the asked member and owners or admins discard it. An open proposal roots its files, never expires, and is erased with its path. A restricted agent's hire through a view asks the owners and admins. The runtime side is P40b.
- The runtime side of saved changes that wait for review (folder core P40b, migration `20260930093000`, SQL tests 320, 926 and 927 extended; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C2.md` §8, `CloudFolderProposals`). At the start of a turn, a folder-v2 company run that can raise a card (a session, not a worker, not a wake from another room) lists its agent's open proposals for its member. When it lists any, the prompt gets a `proposals` block just before `skills` (each change as one plain line, the rest counted), and the run gets `apply_proposal` and `discard_proposal`, which take only a listed id. `apply_proposal` goes through the gate with the proposal's effect; its card shows the changes, never the files (a single change as its own card; more as "Apply N changes saved from …", the first 8 in order, the rest counted). An allow records the review with the proposal, then applies it as one operation and tells the agent what saved, whom it hired and what did not save. A deny closes the proposal as declined through the tool boundary's new optional `denied` hook, which gets the denial's id on every denying path; `discard_proposal` closes it without one. The reviewer reads the whole proposal beside the card (every change with its files' whole parses, and each item a new folder contains by path and kind: a proposal unit's new `contains`), through the tool boundary's new optional `reviewContents` hook, which the gate adds to the action as `contents`; a proposal the list carries only in part is refused, and a hook that cannot give all of it fails the review closed. A member's discard or an erasure between the recorded review and the apply leaves nothing applied: the kernel cancels the call's own receipt and answers `qorpera.folder-proposal-unapplied/v1`, which the operation executor's new `CloudToolOperationSettled` ends as a refusal (failed, `stale_version`), never a receipt that fences the run; one that lands while the apply waits for the proposal leaves a commit that saved nothing under the receipt, which a retry replays and the agent hears as the same "nothing was applied". A change that waits for review is observed as `proposed` (`protected_path`), not `succeeded`: the folder-v2 file tools tell the operation executor before it finishes the observation. Each listed proposal carries its `reads` (every path it names, with the authority the list was decided on); the run releases them with what composition read, under the composed context's hash, before its first model request, so a grant revoked after the listing fails the turn closed or fences the run. A `CloudCompanyFolderRelease`'s `version` may now be null (a path the context names without reading a file). The folder client gains `agentProposals`, `recordProposalReview`, `applyProposal` (which may answer `{unapplied}`) and `closeProposal`; the native host may make the three calls.
- A member's own open proposals and discard, for the web (folder core P40c, migration `20260930102000`, SQL test 937; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C2.md` §8). `qorpera_member_folder_agent_proposals(env, staffAgentId, {cursor?})` (authenticated) lists the caller's open proposals of one agent as the run's list does (oldest first, 4 a page, each with its first 50 units, only while the member may read every path it names), with each structured file's parse cut to the strings `name`, `title`, `role` and `status`. `qorpera_member_folder_discard_proposal(env, proposalId)` (authenticated) is the core's discard as the caller's own actor: the member it waits for, owners and admins; the core's `qorpera_folder_member_discard_proposal` now also takes a member's own actor (`qorpera_folder_caller_allowed`).
- Human-task wakes on folder-v2 (company state files H2c = P31, migration `20260930065000`, SQL tests 915 and 916; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (P31)"): P29's commit hook `qorpera_folder_listener_commit` now writes a pending mark in `qorpera_folder_human_task_wakes` for each task change that should wake the task's `from` agent, made by anyone but that agent: done and declined, accepted, returned and cancelled, a new person, a hand-off (the new `from`) and a new open task (`assigned`); never a tick, a due move, a rename or a restore. The shared folder drain `human_tasks` (`qorpera_folder_human_tasks_due`, `qorpera_folder_human_tasks_company`, one company per transaction in the core's lock order, the tasks FOR SHARE at rank 5) makes the wakes, and one overdue wake per (task, due) of an open or returned task (a partial index by due). A mark whose task moved on is skipped (`changed`); an inactive `from` hands the wake to the Co-Founder. The member who started the task pays through `qorpera_starter_sponsor`; a starter who left, no login that can pay, or a ChatGPT plan leaves the mark `unpaid` (`human-task` is a listener source), until a member who may write chooses to pay with `qorpera_folder_human_task_wake_continue(env, task)`; `qorpera_member_folder_human_task_waits(env)` lists them. The wake is an `agent` wake with source kind `human-task`, no parent run and its task as source; the pin trigger takes its agent, payer, thread and payload from the mark and the task, `qorpera_folder_wake_authorized` checks the mark (and, before the run, the task, agent and payer), the wake policy answers in the agent's own thread when there is one (`continuation`), and `qorpera_folder_read_wake` adds who made the change and the task's path, title, status and due when the run may read its file, recording that read first (once per version shown, with its authority) so the run's later effects recheck it and erasure finds the run.
- The runtime reads a human-task wake (P31): `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` accepts source kind `human-task` with the closed payload `qorpera.human-task-wake/v1` (`taskId`, `reason`, `commitId` or `dueAt`, `task`), no parent run and no routine, and the prompt's wake protocol tells the agent what to do with it.
- Event wakes on folder-v2, the database side (folder core C4, E2a; migration `20260930061000`, SQL tests 913 and 923; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §4).
  - A routine file may say `when: event`, with `hook` (a hook's or mailbox's id) and `only` (null, or 1 to 20 event types). It compiles to W2's listener trigger, so W2's version trigger makes its cursor.
  - Its rules refuse a hook that is not a live hook or mailbox of the company, and a ChatGPT plan as payer. A unit that writes it takes the hook `FOR SHARE` at rank 5, so a save and a hook's removal serialize.
  - On folder-v2, W2's version trigger no longer raises for a removed hook or a plan payer (a move or a handover asks no rules). Such a listener never wakes.
  - `qorpera_folder_wake_authorized` takes event wakes while their hook lives, and `qorpera_folder_read_wake` carries W2's batch.
  - The drain is `qorpera_folder_event_wakes_due` and `qorpera_folder_event_wakes_company`, with W2's key, payload and bounds. A paused company's events wait. The due list names a company only when its step would wake a listener now (a delivery of a type the listener takes, no wake pending, no retry waiting), and a step orders and counts only live listeners, so a cursor whose hook was removed never takes a live listener's place. It stays inert until E2b schedules the `events` drain. E2b also adds the parser's `when: event` and `manage_routine`'s event trigger.
- Check-ins for busy Company Computer shells on folder-v2 (2026-10-03 amendment "one idle rule for every machine", item 5; migration `20260930098000`, SQL test 872; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md` §9). When a command an agent started keeps its shell busy and the agent has not started or typed into a command on that screen for 1, 3 or 7 hours, and then every 24 hours, the agent is asked whether the work still needs it, in the conversation of each run that started a command still running there (for a worker, its steering run). It is a question, never a stop. `qorpera_machine_checkins` keeps one row per question (agents' machines and coding sessions will share it); `qorpera_ask_busy_office_shells(environment, limit)` asks one company's questions, each listing at most 8 commands and 3000 bytes; `qorpera_machine_checkin_current` says whether a question still stands (no later mark for that conversation, the shell still busy, that conversation's commands still running, the same seat holder, no work since). `qorpera_report_runtime_office_shell_activity`'s weekly clean-up of ended commands now keeps the row of an agent's last work on a screen while the agent still runs a command there, so the count never moves back. The shared folder drain `machine_checkins` (`qorpera_folder_machine_checkins_due`, `qorpera_folder_machine_checkins_company`; paused and deleting companies are not due) asks, then sends one wake per ready parent (its run ended and no check-in continuation of it is pending), with up to 8 standing questions; it reads the parent's state again under admission, so a parent gets one continuation at a time. A running parent's questions wait. A stopped parent's questions are suppressed and a gone parent's are undeliverable. The wake has source kind `machine-checkin` and continues the parent run as a `machine-event` wake does: the pin trigger, `qorpera_folder_wake_authorized`, the folder materializer, `qorpera_folder_read_wake`, the wake policy and `qorpera_run_continuation` accept it beside `machine-event`.
- The runtime reads a machine check-in wake: `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` accepts source kind `machine-checkin` with the closed payload `qorpera.machine-checkin/v1` (`parentRunId`, `stillWaiting`, and 1 to 8 `checkins` of `checkinId`, `machineKind`, `since`, `askedAt`, `machine`, `busyHours`, `running`, `more`), its continuation is `qorpera.machine-checkin-continuation/v1`, the prompt's wake protocol tells the agent it may stop the program or leave it, and the shell tool's text says a shell busy for hours brings check-ins.
- Check-ins for busy coding sessions on folder-v2 (2026-10-03 amendment "one idle rule for every machine", item 5; migration `20260930109000`, SQL test 988; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md` §9). When a coding session stays busy (its keeper attached and the guest reported it busy within 90 seconds) for 1, 3 or 7 hours since its last turn ended, and then every 24 hours, the agent is asked whether the work still needs it, in the conversation of the worker's steering run, through the same `qorpera_machine_checkins` rows and `machine_checkins` drain as shells (`machine_kind` `coding`). A session whose worker's report still waits to reach its steering run (`completion_state` `waiting`), an erased or retired worker, a staff member who is not active, and a session with a pending quiet question (`coding-quiet:…`) are not asked. `qorpera_ask_busy_coding_sessions(environment, limit)` asks one company's questions, each naming the session with its repository and branch and listing at most 8 running commands (oldest turn first, 2400 bytes) with the turn that started each; `qorpera_machine_checkin_current` keeps a coding question standing while the session is still busy since the same turn; `qorpera_coding_question_stale` makes an archive from a check-in's run refuse once a new turn started.
- The runtime reads a coding session's check-in: `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` takes `todoKey` and `workerKey` (the keys `manage_worker` takes) on a `machine-checkin` item with `machineKind` `coding` and only there, and its running items carry `turn` instead of `startedAt`. The prompt's wake protocol tells the agent to archive a coding session with `manage_worker` action `archive` using those keys, to leave it once a new turn started, and to leave it running if the work still needs it. The worker tool's text says only a busy program keeps a coding session awake and that a session busy for hours after its last turn brings check-ins.
- Check-ins for busy agents' machines on folder-v2 (2026-10-03 amendment "one idle rule for every machine", item 5; migration `20260930181000`, SQL test 989; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md` §9). A busy look now also keeps when it found the machine busy (`qorpera_company_machines.busy_at`). When a task machine stays busy (it should run, its current placement runs, and a look within 90 seconds found it busy) for 1, 3 or 7 hours since the agent last worked with it (the later of the machine's boot and the agent's last command started there), and then every 24 hours, the agent is asked whether the work still needs it, through the same `qorpera_machine_checkins` rows and `machine_checkins` drain as shells (`machine_kind` `machine`). The whole machine is measured, so it is asked about in each conversation whose runs started a command still running there (a worker's: the conversation that dispatched it); with none, the agent that started the machine is asked in the conversation that started it. An always-on machine (its weekly check-in comes later) and a staff member who is not active are not asked. `qorpera_ask_busy_company_machines(environment, limit)` asks one company's questions, each naming the machine and listing at most 8 of that conversation's running commands (3000 bytes, oldest first, the rest counted) with each command's `commandId`; `qorpera_machine_checkin_current` keeps a machine question standing while the same agent would be asked about it in the same conversation from the same start.
- The runtime reads an agent's machine's check-in: `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` takes `commandId` on each running item of a `machine-checkin` item with `machineKind` `machine` and only there. The prompt's wake protocol tells the agent to cancel such a command with `machine_cancel` (the machine's name and the `commandId`), and to stop a machine with `machine_stop` when its running list is empty (busy outside its commands). `machine_start`'s text says a machine busy for hours brings check-ins.
- A weekly check-in for always-on machines on folder-v2 (2026-10-03 amendment "one idle rule for every machine", item 6; migration `20260930201000`, SQL test 999; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md` §9). An always-on machine never sleeps under the rule; the agent that started it is asked once a week whether the work still needs it, busy or not, counted from its last start (`wanted_at`, which a move to another node keeps and a stop and start resets), in the conversation that started it, through the same `qorpera_machine_checkins` rows and `machine_checkins` drain (`machine_kind` `always-on`, the new kind). Only the latest full week passed is asked, so after downtime or a late drain the agent is asked once, never for each week missed. Nobody is asked when a member started the machine last (`desired_by` `member`) or when the starting agent is no longer active; the machine keeps running and members can stop it. `qorpera_ask_always_on_company_machines(environment, limit)` asks one company's questions (`{machine, weeks}`); `qorpera_machine_checkin_current` keeps such a question standing while the machine is still always-on and wanted running since the same start by the same agent, so a stop, a member's start, a new start or its removal suppress it.
- The runtime reads an always-on machine's weekly question: `parseCloudWakeEnvelope` takes a `machine-checkin` item with `machineKind` `always-on` closed to `checkinId`, `machineKind`, `since`, `askedAt`, `machine` and `weeks` (a whole number from 1), with no `busyHours`, `running` or `more`. The prompt's wake protocol tells the agent it is asked once a week about an always-on machine it started, to stop it with `machine_stop` when the work no longer needs it and otherwise to leave it; `machine_start`'s text says the starting agent is asked once a week.
- SECURITY: the folder commit's bytes check decides possession before it calls a reference malformed (folder core; migration `20260930043000`, SQL test 903; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §3). A file reference whose media type or executable bit disagrees with its stored manifest raised 22023 for anyone, so the right hashes under a wrong type told an actor whether the company stores a file it may not read. Now only an actor who holds the bytes (the platform included) gets 22023; anyone else gets `bytes_unverified`, as for a hash the company does not store.
- Erasing a path erases its conflict copies (folder core slice P18d; migration `20260930045000`, SQL test 909; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-ERASURE.md` §2.5, §13), with P17's copies (20260930042000). A copy lies at its commit's position, or at its commit's time when the commit saved nothing (as phase 2 scrubs that commit's units), so a live root keeps its current life's copies and a copy made after the request stays. `qorpera_folder_erase` counts each root's `conflictCopies` beside its versions, refuses "nothing to erase" only when there are neither, and its copies warning looks at copies on both sides. The step's phase 1 erases them after the versions in the same budget: their bytes go to the hash table, their root's spellings to the needles and their commits' runs to phase 4; the erasure's `counts` gain `conflictCopies`.
- A conflict keeps its bytes on the receipt (folder core slice P17; migration `20260930042000`, SQL test 823; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-CORE.md` §3 step 8, §8): a write or new folder whose version check failed keeps each of its files in `qorpera_folder_conflict_copies` (commit, unit, file number, the path it would have been stored at, and the file reference), only when the whole unit's bytes would have passed the commit's own checks (the proof, the objects step 6 locked, and the actor's possession). A copy roots its manifest and chunks for folder GC, and reads as an old version of its path, so whoever may read the path can fetch it and commit it again. The commit's units JSON is unchanged. A reference that disagrees with its own proof now raises 22023 even when its unit conflicts. No new lock; folder-v2 only.
- Hooks and mailboxes on folder-v2 (folder core C4, E1; migration `20260930060000`, SQL tests 912 and 919; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-EVENTS.md` §3). Creating a hook or a mailbox, and pausing, resuming or removing one, works for folder-v2 companies again, in core §3's order (the fence, the company row KEY SHARE and the member, the hook at rank 5, a per-company advisory lock for the caps). Each is described by a connection file, `connections/hook-<name>.md` or `connections/mailbox-<name>.md`, two new structured kinds whose settings the platform's writer keeps in line with the hook (never its address or a secret) and whose body is the company's; `qorpera_folder_hook_index` indexes them. P22's marks and work gain `subject_kind` (vault, hook, mailbox); the claim answers `subjectKind` (and `body` for a hook or a mailbox) and `qorpera_finish_connection_file` takes `p_subject_kind`. A vault account's file never takes the two prefixes. A write of a hook's file holds the hook `FOR SHARE` at rank 5, so a removal waits for it. A new mail domain marks every folder-v2 mailbox. A mailbox's creation reads the domain `FOR SHARE`, last, so a creation and a domain change serialize.
- `manage_worker` and `report_work` on folder-v2 (company state files F2-5b-4, `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §8 "Plan (F2-5b)" §7; SQL from F2-5b-2): a folder-v2 agent's workers are folders of its todos (`agents/<agent>/todos/<todo>/<worker>`), and the tools write their files as the run through `qorpera_folder_worker_commit`, carrying the worker tool's request so the folder core's hook checks the files and indexes the worker in the same commit (`CloudFolderWorkers`). dispatch makes the todo's folder when missing and the worker's folder with `brief.md`; followup writes the brief with one more follow-up and removes the earlier `results.md`; both carry the automatic review's approval (C2 R1). decide writes the decision into `results.md`, and a worker run's report writes `results.md` in the folder its wake names. cancel calls `qorpera_folder_cancel_agent_worker` (its own receipt); status lists one todo's workers from the worker index (`qorpera_folder_agent_workers`; it needs a todoKey and has no pages) and reads one worker's whole result from its file, released with the result. archive and coding sessions refuse on folder-v2. The folder client gains `workerCommitStaged`, `agentWorkers` and `cancelWorker`; the three RPCs join the path control's list, the gateway's union and the native host's calls. Until now these tools refused on folder-v2 (55000), so no folder-v2 agent could start a worker. With them, migration `20260930075000` (SQL test 840 extended): a follow-up's request names the results it removes (`resultsVersion`), so a decision that landed since the tool read them makes the follow-up a `version_changed` conflict and nothing of it is saved (before, the brief saved and the verify step refused the change set, XX000); and `qorpera_folder_agent_workers` filters (workerKey, state, search) and pages (16 newest first, a cursor, `moreWorkers`, `nextCursor`) in SQL, so a todo with many workers never outgrows one answer.
- An upload holds the bytes its actor put under its own earlier intents (folder core, for F3 step 3's writes through the mount; migration `20260928489290`, SQL test 931; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-FOLDER-WRITES.md`, slice 3a). `qorpera_folder_open_upload` now lists as present an object the catalog has that the same actor (kind and scope) put under another unexpired intent in the same environment, so a big file's chunks, sent while the file was still being written, are not put again when its whole manifest is staged. Same lock and GC mark check as before; an expired intent no longer counts.
- The object gateway's byte route for the folder service (F3 step 3b, writes through the mount; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §8): `PUT /v1/folder-service-objects/<environmentId>/<sha256>` on the folder service's own token (`QORPERA_OBJECT_GATEWAY_FOLDER_SERVICE_TOKEN`; off until it is set, and it must differ from every other gateway token), naming the view's actor (a member, the platform or a view) and its upload intent. It runs a run's put's checks (the intent lists the object for that actor, the hash, then the catalog) through the shared `putFolderObject`. The folder object authority now takes any folder actor, not only a run.
- `manage_routine` on folder-v2 takes a `file_change` trigger (P29's file-change wakes; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md` §2). It writes `when: file-change`, `watch` (the path as given; the folder core folds it), `match` (`exact` or `subtree`) and the time zone into the routine's file, and the save's own rules refuse a watch the agent may not read and a ChatGPT plan as payer. The folder-v2 description now offers it, and says that a routine listens from the save that set it up, that its own changes never wake it, and that `report: required` needs its public thread. Before, every folder-v2 agent was told that file routines were not available, though P29 was live (live check on 10c3ee8c, runs d59f0a93 and 231d7f2c). Event routines stay refused until E2.
- The folder-v2 worker drain (company state files F2-5b-3, migration `20260930003100`, SQL tests 842 and 843; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` "Plan (F2-5b)" §6): `qorpera_folder_workers_due(after, limit)` lists the folder-v2 environments with a due worker, and `qorpera_folder_workers_company(environment, limit ≤ 32)` handles one company in its own transaction. It starts a dispatched or followed-up worker (the dispatch wake, with the todo's file and the worker's folder as its paths; one refused or cancelled at insert fails the worker as `not_started`); fails one whose wake ended without a report; cancels one whose parent stopped (`parent_stopped`) or whose todo was cancelled (`todo_cancelled`); and revives a parent with up to four results, one revival at a time, as the legacy drain does. An erased worker's waiting results are suppressed. It locks in the core's order (the fence, the requesters, agents and execution authorities, then the todos and the workers at rank 5, the parents' conversations, admission last) and answers `paused`, `deleting`, `busy` or `idle` rather than raising. The legacy drain keeps its text. Both functions are the service role's alone.
- Artifacts refuse folder-v2 companies (folder core, from P13's activation checks; migration `20260930041000`, SQL test 902; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §9.3): `qorpera_register_artifact(_v2)`, the runner broker's output objects and artifact registration, and `qorpera_create_artifact_index`, `_create_artifact_retention_hold`, `_complete_object_reachability_graph` and `_cancel_artifact_gc_plan(_v2)` answer 0A000 for a folder-v2 company as their first statement, so none takes the gc fence (the company row FOR UPDATE) through the artifact triggers. No product feature registers artifacts for folder-v2; office capture, export and import already refused. Other companies are unchanged.
- The follow-up queue (folder core slice P15, core C2 "After the commit"; migration `20260930022000`, SQL tests 877–879; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` "Plan (P15)"): a folder-v2 commit now only marks what changed (an agent saved new, archived, retired or reactivated, and a company name to copy), and a pg_cron step every 2 seconds converges one marked subject at a time in its own transaction. A hire gets its default read and write grant on the company's shared folders (one widening authority journal row), its vault grants and connector operations, and its first-run kickoff and turn, authored by the member whose commit made it active. An archive pauses the agent's routines with the new pause reason `agent_archived` and revokes its secret grants, execution authority, bindings and open sessions. The company name in `vision/vision.md` is copied to the company row (`FOR NO KEY UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, core §3's one named exception). Routine firing defers an archived agent's routine and marks the agent. The Co-Founder's first-run turn moves from the creation commit to the queue, and a company is `ready` once its Co-Founder has converged. Service-role kicks: `qorpera_folder_follow_up_now` (one subject) and `qorpera_folder_follow_up_commit` (one commit's subjects, one per call, answering `more`). The legacy staff triggers skip folder-v2 companies.
- `manage_routine` treats `agent_archived` as the platform's pause: an agent active again resumes such a routine with a save of the unchanged file, and the read-back says the routine was paused when its agent was archived (folder core slice P15).
- File-change routines wake their agents on folder-v2 (folder core C4, slice P29a; migration `20260930020000`, SQL tests 887 and 888; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-LISTENER.md`): a routine whose `when` is `file-change` listens from the change set that last saved its file and is woken once per later change set touching its `watch`, never by its own agent's change, never naming or counting a path its run may not read. `qorpera_folder_listen_company` drains one company's journal in its own transaction (fence and ranks, then the cursor lease `FOR UPDATE SKIP LOCKED`, sessions, admission, wakes), a paused company moves on without waking, an archived agent's listener is set aside for P15, and `qorpera_folder_listen_due` lists the companies with work. The wake (`source_kind` `company-file`, `source_id` the commit, key `file:<seq>:<version>`) stores version ids; `qorpera_folder_read_wake` builds `changes` when the run reads it, under the run's read rights, and the materializer cancels a wake none of whose changes the run may still read. The H2c hook pair (`qorpera_folder_listener_targets`, `qorpera_folder_listener_commit`) sees every change set in order; a failed call is recorded and called again on a doubling wait. A coding session's commit during a turn has its turn run as causal parent. Erasure notes the runs a file wake about an erased change started.
- Runs on a folder-v2 company (folder core slice P28 part 1, core C4; migration `20260930018000`, SQL test 874; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-RUNS.md`): `qorpera_folder_materialize_due` lists the folder-v2 companies with due work, and `qorpera_folder_materialize_company` turns one company's queued turn requests (the Co-Founder's first run) and queued wakes (posts, coworker messages, routines) into runs in one transaction, in the core's lock order: the fence and the company row, the members, agents, authorities, rooms and routines, the work rows, the model logins and compute identities, the conversations and inputs, then per item the job and run, the reservation, the transcript events, admission and the queued event (which inserts a turn's first-run or human wake). Jobs and runs no longer require a substrate or snapshot; a run pins `company_seq` (the folder clock's `last_seq`) instead, and has exactly one of the two pins. The runner broker's folder-v2 description carries `company_seq`. The runner-orchestrator edge function gets `folder_materialize_due` and `folder_materialize_company`. Both functions are the service role's alone. Turn requests and wakes share one scan order, and a request behind an earlier one in its conversation, or behind a live turn, waits; an unexpected error undoes only its item and leaves it queued. The reconcile RPC (and so the orchestrator's claim, which calls it) no longer runs the legacy turn, conversation, wake and proposal-review materializers; their functions stay until the legacy-removal sweep. Plain (non-room) conversations keep refusing folder-v2: its conversations are agent threads and group chats.
- Members write their folder-v2 company (folder core C4, slice P34a; migration `20260930021000`, SQL test 898; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md` §5): `qorpera_member_folder_writer(environment)` answers the caller's own member actor for the object gateway's member commit route (refusing non-members, companies being deleted and paused companies), and `qorpera_member_company_state_folders(environment)` lists the live agents' and group chats' files the member may read. Both are `authenticated` reads without a lock; folder-v2 only.
- The runtime reads a folder-v2 run's pin (folder core slice P28b, core C4; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-RUNS.md` §6): the wake envelope's pins accept `companySeq` (a non-negative integer, only beside a null `companySnapshotId`; the database leaves the key out rather than send null), and the runner broker carries a run's `company_seq` as `companySeq`, refusing a run with both pins. SQL sends `pins.companySeq` only after this runtime is live.
- Added erasure of deleted table folders (folder core slice P18c; migration `20260930028000`, SQL test 886; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-ERASURE.md` §12): `tables/` is no longer refused, and the request deletes the retired `qorpera_folder_table_index` rows built from the files it erases, never a live or revived row.
- Added erasure of deleted agent folders, group chats, todos, routines and rules (folder core slice P18b; migration `20260930009300`, SQL test 885; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-ERASURE.md` §11): their rows keep fixed placeholders and bare ids, lose their path and environment, and never come back. An erased agent's execution authority and connector grants are deleted, its path grants are revoked through the authority journal, and its office and browser grants are revoked. The operations log's earlier names, charters and conversation titles (an agent's pair conversations included) are scrubbed, each row's hash recomputed. Speaker names on past messages stay.
- The member's file mode says `folder` for a folder-v2 company, so the web opens its read-only folder page there (folder core slice P33, core C4; migration `20260930013200`, SQL test 836). `qorpera_member_folder_history` bounds its per-change-set query by its cap (`limit v_cap + 1`). Inert until a folder-v2 company exists.
- The members' and clients' hint for a folder-v2 company (folder core slice P30, core C4; migration `20260930013100`, SQL test 835; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md` §3): when the folder clock moves forward, an AFTER UPDATE trigger on `qorpera_folder_clocks` broadcasts `{schema: "qorpera.folder-hint/v1", seq}` as event `folder` on the private topic `qorpera-folder-<environmentId>` (`qorpera_folder_hint_topic`), with a 20 ms lock budget and every failure absorbed. Members of the company receive it, not while it is being deleted (`qorpera_folder_hint_member` in the receive policy); nobody else publishes. Inert until a folder-v2 company exists.
- Added permanent erasure of deleted folder paths (folder core slice P18a; migration `20260930009200`, SQL tests 883 and 884; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-ERASURE.md`, ratified 3 October 2026): `qorpera_folder_erase` lets an owner or admin erase deleted paths of a folder-v2 company (a contact's file, a human task's folder, any deleted path; not `agents/`, `rules/` or `tables/`, which wait for P18b). A path that holds a file again keeps that file and loses only its earlier lives, and a history that ran through moves must be named whole. The request deletes the retired contact, human-task and connection index rows built from those files; a pg_cron job every 5 seconds then deletes their versions, blanks their paths in change sets, scrubs the tool output and reviewed cards of ended runs that read them, and hands their bytes to folder GC; each step works about a second at most and reads at most 4 MiB, going on from a cursor. The reply also names other paths whose history holds the same bytes. Commit attribution, member ids and read records stay. Inert until the member route calls it.
- Members read their folder-v2 company's folder (folder core slice P27a, core C4; migration `20260930013000`, SQL tests 833 and 834; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C4-MEMBERS.md`): `qorpera_member_folder_read`, `_list`, `_changes` (the feed with each change set's `by` and `at`), `_path_history` (a path's versions, newest first), `_history` (the change sets, newest first, with their readable paths) and `_object` (the byte check for the object gateway's member route), all for authenticated members. The core's read, list, feed and byte check now also take an authenticated member's own actor (`qorpera_folder_caller_allowed`), and stay closed to authenticated. Inert until a folder-v2 company exists.
- The version-1 proof of a staged file refuses folder-v2 companies (folder core slice P10, C5 early; migration `20260930001100`, SQL test 821; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1D.md` §9.3 step 3): `qorpera_verify_company_path_file` answers 0A000 for a folder-v2 company before it takes the gc fence, so a refused call takes no lock, and a folder-v2 company's chunk index is complete with no version-1 case. Snapshot-v1 companies are unchanged. Needs P09's fixture move (#724).
- A company being deleted serves its folder to no one (folder core slice P01, F3's P1; migration `20260930001000`, SQL test 820): `qorpera_folder_read_actor` now refuses members and the platform with 55000 'Company is being deleted' once the company has a deletion row, so read, list and the feed stop for them as they already did for runs and views (which keep 'Company execution is paused'). A pause alone still lets members and the platform read. One plain read, no lock; the body carries the marker `20260930001000`.
- A folder-v2 run's memory and skills work through the folder core (folder core slice P20, core C3; migration `20260930001200`, SQL test 822): the notebook and skill composition and `update_memory`, `recall_memory`, `read_skill`, `search_skills` and `update_skill` run unchanged over `CloudFolderNotebooks`, a folder-v2 form of the trusted gateway. Every entry read is recorded; a tool call releases its reads with the run actor under the hash of exactly the text it answers, and the reads that compose the context are released once under the hash of the composed context before the first model request. A change is a one-unit change set under the call's receipt (a change of an absent path is keyed to its call, so a later re-creation is not a replay); a stale version is read again and redone as before. Deleting a skill from the agent's own library records the automatic review's approval of the call, as `company_delete` does: the review recorder takes `update_skill` for deletions under the run agent's own skills folder only. For every company, an `update_skill` delete is now reviewed as destructive. Other runs keep the legacy gateway.
- A folder-v2 run's Company File tools work through the folder core (folder core slice P19, core C3; the 2 October 2026 amendment "light threads", item 2; no migration): `company_read`, `company_list`, `company_write`, `company_mkdir` and `company_delete` act as the run actor. A read is a window of a file (fetching only the chunks it falls in) or what is at a path; a listing is a page of a folder, paged by its last path. Each answer is fitted to one tool result, and everything it shows is released with the run actor under the hash of exactly that text before the model sees it, cited as `companyReadId`. A change is a one-unit change set under the call's receipt (`qorpera.company-folder-result/v1`: `applied`, `proposed` or `not_applied`, with what to do next on a stale version or a missing folder); a deletion records the automatic review's approval of its call, and deletes a file or an empty folder. Other runs keep the legacy tools.
- Added the vault's due marks for connection files (core C3, P22a; migration `20260930006000`, SQL tests 852 and 853; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-STATE-FILES.md` §2 and §8, core §3 step 4 and C4 amended 3 October 2026): on a company that uses the new company folder, every change of a company connection (a catalog connector's or a website account's; not a hook's signing secret or a secret card) that its `connections/<name>.md` file shows (a connection added or disconnected, its account name, an operation granted or withdrawn, a website account's state, login, site, two-step method or todo) inserts a mark in the vault's own transaction, never waiting on anything. A claim never waits for a vault change or a company being deleted. `qorpera_claim_connection_file` and `qorpera_finish_connection_file` (service role) let a writer take one due connection at a time and learn what its file should say, from an allow-list of the vault's fields only (never a secret, an envelope, a key or the vault's own text); the writer itself comes in P22b, in the object gateway and switched off. `qorpera_mark_connection_files(environment)` marks every connection of an environment due at once (an operator step). The vault stays the authority: no tool reads the file to act. No company uses the new folder yet.
- Added the Company Computer to companies on the new company folder (core C1c-3j, P06a; migration `20260930012000`, SQL test 893; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.7): such a company's first computer is registered with `qorpera_register_runtime_office` as its owner (the coordinator does it the first time the company needs one), and a member lists, starts, watches and cancels it with no exclusive company lock: the office paths take the folder fence and the company row only to share, and a start or an activation takes its members and agent before the office, so an agent's return never deadlocks with it. Adopting a replaced computer's screens is refused on such a company (only a legacy computer's are ever adopted). Screens and an agent's own start follow in C1c-3k and 3l. Companies on the old snapshot are unchanged; no company uses the new folder yet.
- The Company Computer's screens, restarts and power-off work on the new company folder (core C1c-3k part 1, P06b; migration `20260930019000`, SQL test 894; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.11): a restart, reset or host-reboot activation takes its start's people before the computer; screen heartbeats, restarts, the agent's screen tool and disposal accept the new folder; a screen's cleanup, observation retention, disposal, a member's new computer and the power-off take no exclusive company lock; a member's start status is plain reads. Adopting a legacy computer's screens stays refused there.
- The Company Computer's access, screens and viewers keep one lock order on the new company folder (core C1c-3k part 2, P06b; migration `20260930019100`, SQL test 895; `deploy/first-node/RUNTIME-COMPANY-FOLDER-C1C.md` §6.12): changing an agent's access takes the agent and every computer in order, so two changes never deadlock; a computer added while an agent is hired gets the agent's access; opening a screen, private heartbeats, viewers and the browser grant take their members before the computer. Access status is plain reads.
